\|f_{1}\|
,
\|\varphi_{1}\|
and
\|\{\varphi_{1}\}_{q}\|
collected in (
9.43
) as well as the assumption (
9.30
),

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{s}_{2}-\frac{1+f_{1}}{2(1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q})^{2}}\right\|=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{(1+f_{1})(2+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}+\varphi_{1})\{\varphi_{1}\}_{q}}{2(1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q})^{2}(1+\varphi_{1})^{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\{\varphi_{1}\}_{q}\|\lesssim q^{2}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}\lesssim\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|.

The proof of Lemma
9.2
is finished.

9.1.3
Quantitative estimates: the Taylor remainder

Having obtained approximations of the leading Taylor coefficients
\widetilde{g}_{1}
and
\widetilde{g}_{2}
of
\widetilde{g}
and
\widetilde{s}_{2}
of the pullback generating function
\widetilde{S}
, we now need to estimate the corresponding Taylor remainders of
\widetilde{g}
.
We need to introduce some additional definitions in the following.

For
\lambda>0
, we define the complex domain
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\subset\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{C}
to be

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}_{1}\times\mathbb{C}\ :\ |\Im x|+\lambda^{-1}|y|<1\}.

If
u
is a function defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, we denote by
|u|^{(\lambda)}
the supremum norm of
u
over
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
If
u
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{r}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, we set

|u|^{(\lambda,r)}:=\sup\bigg\{\biggl|\frac{\partial^{j+k}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k}}(x,y)\biggr|\ :\ (x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda},\ 0\leq j+k\leq r\bigg\}.

(9.44)

The elementary properties of the
|\cdot|^{(\lambda,r)}
-norms that we will need are collected in Appendix
A
.

\Im x
|y|
1
-1
\lambda

Figure 1:
An illustration of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.

The following Lemma is the main result of the current section.

Lemma 9.3
.

Suppose

\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1\quad\text{and}\quad\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|\leq\frac{q^{-6}}{6}.

Then
\widetilde{g}
and
\widetilde{S}
are analytic on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
with
\lambda=q^{-5}/4
.
Furthermore, for
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, we have

\left|{\widetilde{g}(x,y)-\big(\widetilde{g}_{1}(x)y+\widetilde{g}_{2}(x)y^{2}\big)}\right|\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}|y|^{3}

(9.45)

and

\left|{\widetilde{S}(x,y)-\widetilde{s}_{2}(x)y^{2}}\right|\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}|y|^{3}

(9.46)

where
\nu_{0}=64
.

Before proceeding to the proof, we explain the technical issue that necessitates the introduction of the domains of the form
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
The key issue is that the function
\widetilde{g}(\cdot,y)
for a fixed
y\neq 0
is in general less regular than
\phi:\mathbb{T}_{1}\to C
, in the sense that it extends analytically only to a complex domain
\mathbb{T}_{a}
for some
a<1
.

3
3

3

This is analogous to the fact in billiard dynamics that the billiard map on the phase space is generally less smooth than the boundary of the convex billiard domain.

The loss of regularity implies that the Taylor remainder in (
9.5
) has smaller analyticity strip in
x
whereas the leading terms
\widetilde{g}_{j},j=1,2
are analytic over the larger domain
\mathbb{T}_{1}
.
By analyticity, the
k
-th Fourier coefficients of
\widetilde{g}_{j}
decay exponentially faster in
k
than that of the Taylor remainder.
This is potentially a problem because, with
y\approx p/q-\omega
, the
\pm q
-th Fourier coefficient of
\widetilde{g}(\cdot,y)
, which are presumably the largest Fourier coefficients of the resonant part of
\widetilde{g}
, has the size
\sim|\omega-p/q|^{2}\mathrm{e}^{2\pi(a-1)q}
whereas that of the Taylor remainder has the size
\sim|\omega-p/q|^{3}
.
If
\mathrm{e}^{2\pi(a-1)q}\ll|\omega-p/q|
then the uncontrolled error from the Taylor remainder will be much larger than the approximating functions in the estimates (
9.31
) and (
9.32
).
Therefore, it is key to keep track of how fast
a
decreases as
|y|
increases from
0
.
More precisely, we aim to establish the analyticity of
\widetilde{g}
over a domain
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, which by definition has a ‘triangular shape’ in the sense that its
y
-slice is a complex neighborhood
\mathbb{T}_{a}
such that
a
decreases linearly from
1
with a speed
\lambda^{-1}
as
|y|
increases from
0
.
Then, we restrict
\widetilde{g}
and
\widetilde{S}
on the slab
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\cap\{|y-(p/q-\omega)|<|\omega-p/q|/2\}
, where the loss of analyticity width in the
x
-variable is uniformly bounded in
q
by
\lesssim q^{5}|\omega-p/q|\ll 1
.
This allows us to show that the first
N
Fourier coefficients (with
N
not too big relative to
q
) of the Taylor remainders of (
9.5
) and (
9.28
) are much smaller than the counterparts of the leading terms, and hence they can be regarded truly as ‘error terms’.

Another technical issue arises when we consider the Taylor remainders over
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
: it seems not straightforward to show that the remainders vanish to the order
y^{3}
.
If a function is analytic and bounded over a ‘rectangular’ domain like
\mathbb{T}_{1}\times\mathbb{C}
, then it is standard to use Cauchy’s estimate to obtain that the
N
-th order Taylor remainder of the expansion at
y=0
vanishes to the order
y^{N}
.
For functions defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, however, the Cauchy’s estimate is not applicable.
Therefore, we resort to a more rudimentary approach by directly bounding the
\mathcal{C}^{r}
norms (
9.44
) of
\widetilde{g}
and
\widetilde{S}
viewed as a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
function on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
The lengthy but elementary estimates about functions on domains of the form
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
are collected in Appendix
A
.
This approach is probably far from optimal and it is partially responsible for the large size of the exponent
\nu
in the condition (
6.1
) of Theorem
6.1
.

We now begin the proof of Lemma
9.3
.

Chain of domains.

Recall that, by construction,

\widetilde{g}=\big(g+\varphi\circ F-\varphi\big)\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}),\qquad\varphi=f+\psi\circ F-\psi

where
\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}
is the right inverse of
\id+\Psi
.
We shall consider the chain of inclusion of domains schematised as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{g}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g+\varphi\circ F-\varphi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi,f,g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{3}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{0}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

(9.47)

where we shall fix

\lambda_{0}=1,\quad\lambda_{1}=\frac{1}{q^{5}},\quad\lambda_{2}=\frac{1}{2q^{5}},\quad\lambda_{3}=\frac{1}{3q^{5}}\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{4}=\frac{1}{4q^{5}}.

In the diagram (
9.47
), the vertical arrows with dotted lines denote domains of definitions.
We will show
\widetilde{g}
and
\widetilde{S}
are well-defined analytic functions on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{3}}
and establish a uniform
\mathcal{C}^{3}
bound

|\widetilde{g}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}\quad\text{and}\quad|\widetilde{S}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}.

(9.48)

with a universal constant
\nu_{0}=64
.

In fact, the estimate (
9.48
) is sufficient for us to deduce (
9.45
) and (
9.46
) of Lemma
9.3
.
Indeed, by the usual Taylor remainder formula,

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}(x,y)=\widetilde{g}_{1}(x)y+\widetilde{g}_{2}(x)y^{2}+\mathcal{Q}_{1}(x,y);\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{1}(x,y)=\frac{y^{3}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial^{3}\widetilde{g}}{\partial y^{3}}(x,ty)(1-t)^{2}dt;

\displaystyle\tilde{S}(x,y)=\tilde{s}_{2}(x)y^{2}+\mathcal{Q}_{2}(x,y),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2}(x,y)=\frac{y^{3}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial^{3}\tilde{S}}{\partial y^{3}}(x,ty)(1-t)^{2}dt.

For
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{4}}
, by the explicit expressions above and (
9.48
),

|\mathcal{Q}_{j}(x,y)|\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}|y|^{3},\qquad(j=1,2).

We readily obtain (
9.45
) and (
9.46
).

In the rest of this section, we focus on proving that all the compositions in (
9.47
) are well-defined and the estimate (
9.48
) holds.

Estimates of
\overline{f}
and
\overline{g}
.

Recall that the functions
f
and
g
in the formula
F(x,y)=(x+\omega+y+f(x,y),y+g(x,y))
are determined by
(\omega,\phi)
via (
5.6
) and that
f(x,0)=g(x,0)=0
for all
x
so that
F
preserves the real torus
\mathbb{T}\times\{0\}
.
By our choice of
\psi
in the preceding section and by (
9.3
), we clearly have
\varphi(x,0)=0
as well.
We may therefore put

f(x,y)=y\overline{f}(x,y),\quad g(x,y)=y\overline{g}(x,y),\quad\psi(x,y)=y\overline{\psi}(x,y),\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi(x,y)=y\overline{\varphi}(x,y)

for some
\overline{f}
,
\overline{g}
,
\overline{\psi}
and
\overline{\varphi}
analytic in a complex neighbourhood of
\mathbb{T}\times\{0\}
.

Let us first focus on
\overline{f}
and
\overline{g}
.
Substituting the definitions of
\overline{f}
and
\overline{g}
into (
5.6
) and rearranging,

\begin{gathered}1+\overline{f}(x,y)+\frac{\phi(x+\omega+y(1+\overline{f}(x,y)))-\phi(x+\omega)}{y}=\frac{1}{1+\dot{\phi}(x)}\\
1+\overline{g}(x,y)=\big[1+\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+y(1+\overline{f}(x,y)))\big]\bigg[1+\overline{f}(x,y)+\frac{\phi(x+y(1+\overline{f}(x,y)))-\phi(x)}{y}\bigg].\end{gathered}

(9.49)

By the Mean Value Theorem,

\displaystyle\frac{\phi(x+\omega+y(1+\overline{f}(x,y)))-\phi(x+\omega)}{y}=

\displaystyle(1+\overline{f}(x,y))\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+t(y+f(x,y)))dt

\displaystyle\frac{\phi(x+y(1+\overline{f}(x,y)))-\phi(x)}{y}=

\displaystyle(1+\overline{f}(x,y))\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+t(y+f(x,y)))dt.

Hence, Equation (
9.49
) can be written as

\displaystyle\overline{f}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\bigg[(1+\dot{\phi}(x))\Big(1+\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+t(y+f(x,y)))dt\Big)\bigg]^{-1}-1

(9.50)

\displaystyle\overline{g}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{(1+\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+y+f(x,y)))\big(1+\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+t(y+f(x,y)))dt\big)}{(1+\dot{\phi}(x))\big(1+\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+t(y+f(x,y)))dt\big)}-1.

Using (
9.49
) and (
9.50
), we control the norms of
\bar{f}
and
\bar{g}
over domains
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
for a sufficiently small
\lambda
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Under the condition
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
, the functions
\bar{f}
and
\bar{g}
are well-defined on the domain
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
with
\lambda=(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}
, and

|\bar{f}|^{(\lambda)}\leq\frac{|\dot{\phi}|(2-|\dot{\phi}|)}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}},\quad|1+\bar{f}|^{(\lambda)}\leq\frac{1}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad|\bar{g}|^{(\lambda)}\leq\frac{2|\dot{\phi}|(2+|\dot{\phi}|)}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}}.

(9.51)

Proof.

We use (
9.49
) and the contraction mapping principle to solve for
\bar{f}
.
Let
\lambda=(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}
and define
r=\lambda^{-1}-1
.
Fix
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and consider the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{x,y}:\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{r}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{C}

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle\mapsto-\frac{\dot{\phi}(x)}{1+\dot{\phi}(x)}-\frac{\phi(x+\omega+y(1+z))-\phi(x+\omega)}{y}.

By definitions of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and
r
, when
|z|\leq r
we have
|\Im(x+\omega+y(1+z))|\leq|\Im x|+\lambda^{-1}|y|<1
.
Hence, the map
\mathcal{P}_{x,y}
is well-defined on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{r}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{x,y}^{\prime}(z)=-\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+y(1+z))
.
Since the assumption
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
implies
a fortiori
that
|\dot{\phi}|<1
, the map
\mathcal{P}_{x,y}
is contracting over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{r}
.
On the other hand, by the mean value theorem and the definitions of
\lambda
and
r
, for
|z|\leq r
,

|\mathcal{P}_{x,y}(z)|\leq\frac{|\dot{\phi}|}{1-|\dot{\phi}|}+(1+r)|\dot{\phi}|=\Big(\frac{1}{1-|\dot{\phi}|}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\Big)|\dot{\phi}|=\frac{2-|\dot{\phi}|}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}}|\dot{\phi}|=\frac{1}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}}-1=r.

It follows that
\mathcal{P}_{x,y}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{r})\subset\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{r}
.
Hence, by the contracting mapping principle there exists a unique fixed point
\mathcal{P}_{x,y}(z)=z
in
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{r}
depending analytically on
(x,y)
.
We shall define
\bar{f}(x,y)
to be this fixed point.
In particular, we have

|\bar{f}(x,y)|\leq r=\frac{|\dot{\phi}|(2-|\dot{\phi}|)}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad|1+\bar{f}(x,y)|\leq 1+r=\frac{1}{\lambda}=\frac{1}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}}.

We have obtained the first two inequalities of (
9.51
).
Finally, from the second line of (
9.50
), we deduce that
g
is also well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and satisfies

|\bar{g}(x,y)|\leq\frac{2|\dot{\phi}|(2+|\dot{\phi}|)}{(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}}.

The proof of the lemma is finished.
∎

Next, we apply Proposition
A.2
to estimate the norms
|\bar{f}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}
and
|\bar{g}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}
by shrinking
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
to a smaller domain
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.

Corollary 9.5
.

Suppose
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
.
Then

|\bar{f}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}+|\bar{g}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}\lesssim 1\quad\text{for}\quad\lambda^{\prime}=(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}/2.

Proof.

To estimate
|\bar{f}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}
and
|\bar{g}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}
, let us define
\Sigma(x,y)=(0,f(x,y))
and

\displaystyle w_{1}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\bigg[(1+\dot{\phi}(x))\Big(1+\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+ty)dt\Big)\bigg]^{-1}-1,

\displaystyle w_{2}(x,y)=

\displaystyle\frac{(1+\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+y))\big(1+\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+ty)dt\big)}{(1+\dot{\phi}(x))\big(1+\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+ty)dt\big)}-1.

By (
9.50
),

\bar{f}=w_{1}\circ(\id+\Sigma)\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{g}=w_{2}\circ(\id+\Sigma).

Observe that for
0\leq t\leq 1
, the expression
\dot{\phi}(x+\omega+ty)
is well-defined for
|\Im x|+|y|<1
.
Hence
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
are well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
.
On the other hand, for
\lambda=(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}
and
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
,
the results of Lemma
9.4
imply that
|\Im x|+|y+f(x,y)|\leq|\Im x|+(|1+\bar{f}|^{(\lambda)})|y|\leq|\Im x|+\lambda^{-1}|y|<1
with
0\leq t\leq 1
, and thus
(\id+\Sigma)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})\subset\mathcal{D}_{1}
and the map
w_{j}\circ(\id+\Sigma)
is well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
Moreover, since the assumption
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
implies
|\dot{\phi}|\leq(2\pi)^{-2}
, computing the derivatives of
w_{j},\ j=1,2
shows that the following crude bounds hold

|w_{j}|^{(1,1)}\lesssim 1+\|\ddot{\phi}\|\lesssim 1\quad\text{and}\quad|w_{j}|^{(1,2)}\lesssim 1+\|\dddot{\phi}\|\lesssim 1.

(9.52)

Let us now apply Proposition
A.2
part 3 with
v_{1}=0
,
v_{2}=w_{1}
,
\bar{\sigma}_{1}=0
,
\bar{\sigma}_{2}=\bar{f}
and
r=2
.
We have accordingly that for all
\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda=(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}
,

4
4

4

The proposition is applied with
(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime})\leftarrow(1,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})
.

|\bar{f}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}\leq C_{2}\Big(\frac{2+\lambda}{1-(\lambda^{\prime}(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{-2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{A_{2}}(1+|w_{1}|^{(1,1)})^{B_{2}}|w_{1}|^{(1,2)}.

Fixing
\lambda^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}
and using (
9.52
), we have

|\bar{f}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}\lesssim 1.

(9.53)

At last, applying Proposition
A.2
part 2 to
\bar{g}=w_{2}\circ(\id+\Sigma)
and using (
9.52
), (
9.53
) and the assumption
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
, we have

|\bar{g}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)}\lesssim|w_{2}|^{(1,2)}(1+|\bar{f}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},2)})^{2}\lesssim 1.

The proof is finished.
∎

We now consider the last two arrows of (
9.47
).

Since
\lambda_{1}<(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}
, it follows from Lemma
9.4
that
F
is well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{1}}
.

We first derive an extension to Proposition
A.2
part (1) and part (2) in order to verify that the last two arrows of (
9.47
) make sense and thus the compositions
\psi\circ F
and
\varphi\circ F
are well-defined.
Let decompose
F=\tau_{\omega}\circ F_{0}
where
F_{0}(x,y)=(x+y+f(x,y),y+g(x,y))
and
\tau_{\omega}(x,y)=(x+\omega,y)
.
Since
\tau_{\omega}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})=\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
for any
\lambda>0
, we just need to verify
F_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{2}})\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{1}}
and
F_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{1}})\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{0}}
.

Fix
q\geq 2
.
By the estimates in Lemma
9.4
for
|\overline{f}|^{\lambda}
and
|\overline{g}|^{\lambda}
and the smallness of
|\dot{\phi}|
, one easily verifies that the condition (
A.5
) of Proposition
A.2
is satisfied with
\bar{\sigma}_{1}=1+\bar{f}
,
\bar{\sigma}_{2}=\bar{g}
and
(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda)\in\{(q^{-5},1),\;(q^{-5}/2,q^{-5})\}
.
Thus, we may apply Proposition
A.2
part (1) with
\id+\Sigma=F_{0}
, which implies the desired inclusion.

Having verified that compositions
\psi\circ F
and
\varphi\circ F
are well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{1}}
and on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{2}}
respectively, we apply Proposition
A.2
part (2) with
\sigma_{1}(x,y)=y+f(x,y)
,
\sigma_{2}(x,y)=g(x,y)
,
v=\psi\circ\tau_{\omega}
and
v=\varphi\circ\tau_{\omega}
to get

\begin{cases}|\psi\circ F|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}=|\psi\circ\tau_{\omega}\circ F_{0}|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}\lesssim|\psi\circ\tau_{\omega}|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}(1+|y+f|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}+|g|^{(\lambda_{1},3)})^{3}\\
|\varphi\circ F|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}=|\varphi\circ\tau_{\omega}\circ F_{0}|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}\lesssim|\varphi\circ\tau_{\omega}|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}(1+|y+f|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}+|g|^{(\lambda_{2},3)})^{3}.\end{cases}

Using Lemma
A.1
with
(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda)=(\lambda_{1},2\lambda_{1})
, we have

1+|y+f|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}+|g|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}\leq\frac{3+\lambda_{1}}{1-1/2}(1+|1+\bar{f}|^{(2\lambda_{1},2)}+|\bar{g}|^{(2\lambda_{1},2)}),

where, since
2\lambda_{1}<(1-|\dot{\phi}|)^{2}/2
, Corollary
9.5
implies that the terms inside the parentheses in the preceding expression is
\lesssim 1
.
We also have the obvious equalities
|\psi\circ\tau_{\omega}|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}=||\psi|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}
and
|\varphi\circ\tau_{\omega}|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}=||\varphi|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}
.
It follows that

|\psi\circ F|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}\lesssim|\psi|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}\quad\text{and}\quad|\varphi\circ F|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}\lesssim|\varphi|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}.

(9.54)

By the same argument using Lemma
A.1
,

|f|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}+|g|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}\lesssim 1.

(9.55)

Estimates of
\psi
and
\varphi
.

Recall that
\bar{\psi}(x,y)=\psi_{1}(x)+\psi_{2}(x)y
by construction.
Applying Lemma
A.1
twice and using
\|\ddot{\psi}_{1}\|\lesssim q^{2}
and
\|\dot{\psi}_{2}\|\lesssim q^{4}
from (
9.43
), we have

|\psi|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}\lesssim|\bar{\psi}|^{(2\lambda_{0},2)}\lesssim\|\ddot{\psi}_{1}\|+\|\dot{\psi}_{2}\|\lesssim q^{4}.

(9.56)

Using the definition
\varphi=f+\psi\circ F-\psi
and applying (
9.54
) - (
9.56
), we obtain

|\varphi|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}\lesssim|f|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}+|\psi|^{(\lambda_{0},3)}\lesssim q^{4}.

(9.57)

Since
\varphi(x,y)=y\bar{\varphi}(x,y)
,
by the mean value theorem,

\bar{\varphi}(x,y)=\varphi_{1}(x)+y\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi}{\partial y^{2}}(x,ty)(1-t)dt.

(9.58)

When
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{1}}
, we have
|y|\leq q^{-5}
.
Therefore, the estimate (
9.57
) together with
\|\varphi_{1}\|<1/18
and
\|\ddot{\varphi}_{1}\|\lesssim q^{2}
from (
9.43
) implies that, for some universal constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle|\bar{\varphi}|^{(\lambda_{1})}\leq

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{1}\|+\frac{1}{2q^{5}}|\varphi|^{(\lambda_{1},2)}<\frac{1}{18}+\frac{c}{q};

(9.59)

\displaystyle|\bar{\varphi}|^{(\lambda_{2},2)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\ddot{\varphi}_{1}\|+\left|{\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi}{\partial y^{2}}}\right|^{(\lambda_{1},1)}\lesssim q^{2}+|\varphi|^{(\lambda_{1},3)}\lesssim q^{4}.

In the second line, we have applied Lemma
A.1
to the integral term of (
9.58
).

Estimates of
\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}
.

Next, we consider the map
\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}
in (
9.47
).
To apply Proposition
A.3
with
\bar{\xi}_{1}\leftarrow\bar{\psi}
,
\bar{\xi}_{2}\leftarrow\bar{\varphi}
,
\Xi\leftarrow\Psi
and
\Sigma\leftarrow\widetilde{\Sigma}
, we need to verify the condition (
A.9
) holds with
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda_{3}
,
\lambda=\lambda_{2}
and
q
sufficiently large, but this follows easily from (
9.56
) and (
9.59
) as well as the definitions of
\lambda_{3}
and
\lambda_{2}
.

Accordingly, Proposition
A.3
applies and the right inverse
\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}
of
\id+\Psi
exists and is defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{3}}
.
By Proposition
A.2
part (1) one also verifies that
(\id+\Psi)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{4}})\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{3}}
for
q
sufficiently large.
Hence, Proposition
A.3
conclusion
A.11
implies that
(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})\circ(\id+\Psi)=\id
on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{4}}
.
If we put
\widetilde{F}=(\id+\Psi)\circ F\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})
, then in view of (
9.47
) the map
\widetilde{F}
is well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{3}}
and we have
(\id+\Psi)\circ F=\widetilde{F}\circ(\id+\Psi)
on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{4}}
.
Write
\widetilde{F}(x,y)=(x+\omega+y+\widetilde{f}(x,y),y+\widetilde{g}(x,y))
for analytic
\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{3}}\to\mathbb{C}
.
Then by the discussion in the beginning of Section
9
we have
\widetilde{f}\circ(\id+\Psi)=0
and
\widetilde{g}=(g+\varphi\circ F-\varphi)\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})
.
By analyticity
\widetilde{f}
vanishes identically.

Write
\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,y)=(y\bar{\sigma}_{1}(x,y),y\bar{\sigma}_{2}(x,y))
.
By the conclusion (
A.12
) of Proposition
A.3
with
r=2
and
\lambda^{\prime\prime}=7\lambda_{4}/6
,

1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\frac{7}{6}\lambda_{4},2)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\frac{7}{6}\lambda_{4},2)}\leq C_{2}\Big(\frac{2+\lambda_{3}}{1-\beta}\Big)^{A_{2}}\Big(\frac{1+|\bar{\psi}|^{(\lambda_{2},1)}+|\bar{\varphi}|^{(\lambda_{2},1)}}{1-|\bar{\varphi}|^{(\lambda_{2})}}\Big)^{B_{2}}\big(1+|\bar{\psi}|^{(\lambda_{2},2)}+|\bar{\varphi}|^{(\lambda_{2},2)}\big)

where
\beta=(\frac{7}{6}\frac{\lambda_{4}}{\lambda_{3}})^{1/2}=(7/8)^{1/2}<1
.
By (
9.56
) and (
9.59
), we arrive at a crude bound:

1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\frac{7}{6}\lambda_{4},2)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\frac{7}{6}\lambda_{4},2)}\lesssim q^{4(B_{2}+1)}.

(9.60)

Estimate of
\widetilde{g}
.

Finally, we may bound
|\widetilde{g}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}
as follows.

\displaystyle|\widetilde{g}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}

\displaystyle=|(g+\varphi\circ F-\varphi)\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}

\displaystyle\lesssim|g+\varphi\circ F-\varphi|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}(1+|\sigma_{1}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}+|\sigma_{2}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)})^{3}

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:tri comp} part (2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim|g+\varphi\circ F-\varphi|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}(1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\frac{7}{6}\lambda_{4},2)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\frac{7}{6}\lambda_{4},2)})^{3}

\displaystyle\text{by Lemma~\ref{lem:tri cauchy}}

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{12(B_{2}+1)}|g+\varphi\circ F-\varphi|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:barsig}}

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{12(B_{2}+1)}(|g|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}+|\varphi|^{(\lambda_{1},3)})

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:psiphi o F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{12(B_{2}+1)}q^{4}

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:fg lam} and \eqref{eq:phi lam}}.

By the explicit recursive formula for
B_{r}
at the end of the proof of
A.2
, one can readily compute that
B_{2}=4
.
Therefore, we have obtained
|\widetilde{g}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}\lesssim q^{64}
, which is the first inequality of (
9.48
).

Estimate of
\widetilde{S}
.

We start by estimating the
|\cdot|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}
-norm of the normalized generating function
S
associated with
(\omega,\phi)
in adapted coordinates.
By (
5.12
),

\frac{\partial S}{\partial x}=y\Big[(1+\overline{g})\Big(1+\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\Big)-1\Big]\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\partial S}{\partial y}=y(1+\overline{g})\Big(1+\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\Big).

It follows from Lemma
A.1
, Corollary
9.5
and (
9.55
) that

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\partial S}{\partial x}}\right|^{(\lambda_{2},2)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|{(1+\overline{g})\Big(1+\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\Big)-1}\right|^{(\lambda_{1},1)}\lesssim(1+|\overline{g}|^{(\lambda_{1},1)})\Big(1+\left|{\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}}\right|^{(\lambda_{1},1)}\Big)\lesssim 1

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\partial S}{\partial y}}\right|^{(\lambda_{2},2)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|{(1+\overline{g})\Big(1+\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\Big)}\right|^{(\lambda_{1},1)}\lesssim(1+|\overline{g}|^{(\lambda_{1},1)})\Big(1+\left|{\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}}\right|^{(\lambda_{1},1)}\Big)\lesssim 1.

Applying the second estimate above to the Taylor remainder of

S(x,y)=\frac{1+f_{1}(x)}{2}y^{2}+y^{3}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial^{3}S}{\partial y^{3}}(x,ty)(1-t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t

and using
\|f_{1}\|\lesssim 1
, we get
|S|^{(\lambda_{2})}\lesssim 1
and thus,

|S|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}\leq\max\bigg\{|S|^{(\lambda_{2})},\left|{\frac{\partial S}{\partial x}}\right|^{(\lambda_{2},2)},\left|{\frac{\partial S}{\partial y}}\right|^{(\lambda_{2},2)}\bigg\}\lesssim 1.

(9.61)

It follows that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{S}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}\lesssim

\displaystyle|S|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}(1+|\sigma_{1}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}+|\sigma_{2}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)})^{3}

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:tri comp} part (2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|S|^{(\lambda_{2},3)}(1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(7\lambda_{4}/6,\ 2)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(7\lambda_{4}/6,\ 2)})^{3}

\displaystyle\text{by Lemma \ref{lem:tri cauchy}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle q^{12(B_{2}+1)}

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:barsig} and \eqref{eq:|S| lam}.}

Using
B_{2}=4
we obtain
|\widetilde{S}|^{(\lambda_{4},3)}\lesssim q^{60}
, which obviously implies the second inequality of (
9.48
).
In view of the discussion immediately following (
9.48
), we have finished the proof of Lemma
9.2
.

9.1.4
End of the proof of Proposition
9.1

We now complete the proof of Proposition
9.1
.
Let us fix from now onwards that
\lambda=q^{-5}/4
and let
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and the
|\cdot|^{(\lambda,r)}
-norm be defined as in the beginning of Section
9.1.1
.
Take
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
Combining Lemma
9.2
and Lemma
9.3
,

\begin{gathered}\bigg|\widetilde{g}(x,y)-y\Big(\omega-\frac{p}{q}+\frac{y}{2}\Big)\frac{\langle\dot{f}_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}\bigg|\lesssim q^{2}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right||y|+q^{5}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right||y|^{2}+q^{\nu_{0}}|y|^{3}\\
\bigg|\widetilde{S}(x,y)-\frac{1+f_{1}(x)}{2(1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}(x))^{2}}y^{2}\bigg|\lesssim\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right||y|^{2}+q^{\nu_{0}}|y|^{3}.\end{gathered}

(9.62)

Recall that in the preceding paragraph we have shown
(\id+\Psi)\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})=\id
over
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and

(\id+\Psi)\circ F\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}):\begin{pmatrix}x\\
y\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}x+\omega+y\\
y+\widetilde{g}(x,y)\end{pmatrix}

is analytic on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
Define a translation
\tau(x,y)=(x,p/q-\omega+y)
.
Then we have

\tau^{-1}\circ(\id+\Psi)\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})\circ\tau=\id\quad\text{over}\quad\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}.

(9.63)

Using assumption (
9.7
), one readily checks that
\mathbb{T}_{1-q^{-1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2}\subset\tau^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
We prove the conclusion of Proposition
9.1
with the following choices.

\Theta:=(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})\circ\tau;\quad\widetilde{F}_{q}:=\tau^{-1}\circ(\id+\Psi)\circ F\circ\Theta;\quad\widetilde{S}_{q}:=S\circ\Theta;\quad\widetilde{g}_{q}:=\widetilde{g}\circ\tau.

(9.64)

Then
\widetilde{F}_{q}(x,y)=(x+p/q+y,y+\widetilde{g}_{q}(x,y))
for
\widetilde{g}_{q}:\mathbb{T}_{1-q^{-1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2}\to\mathbb{C}
analytic.

Let us fix
(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}_{1-q^{-1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2}
.

Define
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}:\mathbb{T}_{1-q^{-1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2}\to\mathbb{C}
as in (
9.8
).
By (
9.63
) and the definition of
\Theta
, we have
(\id+\Psi)\circ\Theta=\tau
.
Componentwise, this is means that

\begin{cases}\theta_{1}(x,y)+\psi(x+\theta_{1}(x,y),\;y+p/q-\omega+\theta_{2}(x,y))=0\\
\theta_{2}(x,y)+\varphi(x+\theta_{1}(x,y),\;y+p/q-\omega+\theta_{2}(x,y))=0.\end{cases}

(9.65)

Since
\Theta(x,y)\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{2}}
, by the first line of (
9.59
) and
\|\phi_{1}\|<1/18
, we have

|\varphi(x+\theta_{1}(x,y),\;y+p/q-\omega+\theta_{2}(x,y))|<\frac{1}{18}|y+p/q-\omega+\theta_{2}(x,y)|

for
q
sufficiently large.
Combining with the second line of (
9.65
) and using
|y|<|\omega-p/q|/2
,

|\theta_{2}(x,y)|<\frac{1}{18}\Big(\frac{3}{2}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|+|\theta_{2}(x,y)|\Big).

The second estimate of (
9.9
) follows from above.
Recall that
\psi(x,y)=y\psi_{1}(x)+y^{2}\psi_{2}(x)
and from (
9.43
) we have
\|\psi_{1}\|\lesssim q
and
\|\psi_{2}\|\lesssim q^{3}
.
Therefore the first line of (
9.65
) and
|\theta_{2}(x,y)|\lesssim|\omega-p/q|
imply

|\theta_{1}(x,y)|\lesssim q\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|.

We have used
|\omega-p/q|\leq q^{-6}
. The proof of (
9.9
) is complete.

Using
|p/q-\omega+y|\lesssim|\omega-p/q|
and (
9.62
),

\begin{gathered}\bigg|\widetilde{g}_{q}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega+y\Big)\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega-y\Big)\frac{\langle\dot{f}_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}\bigg|\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{3}\\
\bigg|\widetilde{S}_{q}(x,y)-\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega+y\Big)^{2}\frac{1+f_{1}(x)}{2(1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}(x))^{2}}\bigg|\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{3},\end{gathered}

which give (
9.10
).
It is clear from the construction that the change of coordinates
\id+\widetilde{\Sigma}
depends analytically on
\phi
and it is real-analytic when
\phi
is so.

Recall that
\Sigma(x,y)=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))=(y\bar{\sigma}_{1}(x,y),y\bar{\sigma}_{2}(x,y))
.
By (
9.60
), Lemma
A.1
and
B_{2}=4
,
\lambda=\lambda_{4}
,

|\bar{\sigma}_{j}|^{(\lambda,2)}\lesssim q^{20}\qquad(j=1,2).

(9.66)

Since the original map
F
preserves the area form
\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y
, the map
\widetilde{F}_{q}
defined by (
9.64
) preserves the area form
\Theta^{*}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y=\tau^{*}(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})^{*}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y
, where

(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})^{*}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y=\Big(1+\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}+\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial x}\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}-\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial y}\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial x}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

Hence we may write

\Theta^{*}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y=\Big(\frac{1}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}}+\widetilde{\eta}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

where

\widetilde{\eta}=\frac{1}{1+\varphi_{1}}-\frac{1}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}}+\tau^{*}\bigg[\Big(\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}(x,y)+1-\frac{1}{1+\varphi_{1}(x)}\Big)+\Big(\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial x}\Big(1+\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}\Big)-\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial y}\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial x}\Big)\bigg].

Recall that the construction in Section
9.1.1
gives
\varphi_{1}=\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}+\{\varphi_{1}\}_{q}
and we have an upper bound
\|\{\varphi_{1}\}_{q}\|\lesssim q^{2}|\omega-p/q|^{2}
from (
9.43
).
Therefore, it can be verified with mean value theorem that

\left\|\frac{1}{1+\varphi_{1}}-\frac{1}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}}\right\|\lesssim q^{2}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

On the other hand, Taylor expanding the
y
-component of the equality
\widetilde{\Sigma}+(\id+\Psi)\circ(\id+\widetilde{\Sigma})=0
we have
\partial\sigma_{2}/\partial y(x,0)=(1+\varphi_{1}(x))^{-1}-1
.
By (
9.66
) and
\sigma_{j}(x,0)=0
, we have that, for
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
,

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}(x,y)+1-\frac{1}{1+\varphi_{1}(x)}}\right|\lesssim|y||\sigma_{2}|^{(\lambda,2)}\lesssim q^{20}|y|,

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\partial\sigma_{j}}{\partial x}(x,y)}\right|\lesssim|y||\sigma_{j}|^{(\lambda,2)}\lesssim q^{20}|y|\quad\text{and}\quad\left|{\frac{\partial\sigma_{j}}{\partial y}(x,y)}\right|\lesssim|\sigma_{j}|^{(\lambda,1)}\lesssim q^{20}.

Since the translation
\tau
maps
(\mathbb{T}_{1-q^{-1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2})
into
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, applying the above estimates to
\widetilde{\eta}
over the domain
\mathbb{T}_{1-q^{-1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2}
, we get

|\widetilde{\eta}|_{1-\frac{1}{q},\frac{1}{2}|\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\lesssim q^{40}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|.

Since
40<\nu_{0}
, we have proved (
9.11
).
The proof of Proposition
9.1
is finished.

9.2
Step 2: coordinate changes by an iterative scheme

Proposition 9.6
.

We consider the same setup as Proposition
9.1
.
Fix
\nu>\nu_{0}
,
M\geq 1
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
.
Then for all
q\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
sufficiently large and satisfying

\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|\leq q^{-\nu},

(9.67)

and with
\widetilde{F}_{q}=\Theta^{-1}\circ F\circ\Theta:(x,y)\mapsto(x+p/q+y,y+\widetilde{g}_{q}(x,y))
denoting the standard map in the coordinates constructed in Proposition
9.1
, the following conclusion holds true.

There exists a further change of coordinates

\id+\Sigma:\mathbb{T}_{1-\frac{2}{q}}\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{3}|\omega-\frac{p}{q}|}\to\mathbb{T}_{1-\frac{1}{q}}\times\mathbb{D}_{\frac{1}{2}|\omega-\frac{p}{q}|}

such that the map
F_{q}:=(\id+\Sigma)^{-1}\circ\widetilde{F}_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma)
takes the form

(x,y)\mapsto(x+p/q+y,y+g_{q}(x,y))

for some analytic
g_{q}

satisfying

\displaystyle|g_{q}|_{1-\frac{2}{q},\frac{1}{3}|\omega-\frac{p}{q}|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}

(9.68)

\displaystyle|\{g_{q}\}_{q}|_{1-\frac{2}{q},\frac{1}{3}|\omega-\frac{p}{q}|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mq}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}

(9.69)

\displaystyle\|\langle g_{q}(\cdot,0)\rangle_{q}^{N}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}(\cdot,0)\rangle_{q}^{N}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\mathrm{e}^{4\pi\kappa}q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}\quad\text{where}\quad N=\kappa q.

(9.70)

Moreover, writing
\Sigma(x,y)=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
for analytic
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}:\mathbb{T}_{1-2/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/3}\to\mathbb{C}
, we have

|\sigma_{j}|_{1-\frac{2}{q},\frac{1}{3}|\omega-\frac{p}{q}|}\lesssim q^{5+\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2},\qquad(j=1,2).

(9.71)

Remark
.

The ‘sufficiently large’ condition on
q
depends only on
\nu
,
M
and
\kappa
.
In particular, it is independent of the map
F
as long as
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
.
If the irrationality exponent of
\omega
is
\geq\nu
, then there exist infinitely many
q
satisfying (
9.67
).

9.2.1
An iterative lemma for Proposition
9.6

Let us consider generally a map

F:(x,y)\mapsto(x+p/q+y,y+g(x,y))

(9.72)

for analytic
g:\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}\to\mathbb{C}
.
We consider a change of coordinates
\Phi=\id+\Psi
where
\Psi(x,y)=(\psi(x,y),\varphi(x,y))
and
\varphi=\psi\circ F-\psi
.
A straightforward calculation analogous to that in Section
9.1.1
shows that, in the new coordinates, the transformed map
\Phi\circ F\circ\Phi^{-1}
writes

\Phi\circ F\circ\Phi^{-1}(x,y)=(x+p/q+y,y+\widetilde{g}(x,y))

where
\widetilde{g}
is an analytic function satisfying

\widetilde{g}\circ\Phi(x,y)=g+\psi\circ F^{2}-2\psi\circ F+\psi.

(9.73)

In the following lemma, we show that a judicious choice of
\psi
can reduce the nonresonant part of the map in the new coordinates in the sense that
\widetilde{g}
has a ‘smaller’ resonant part compared with
g
.

To state this result, we need to introduce a new norm on the space of analytic functions: for
u:\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}\to\mathbb{C}
analytic, let us define

{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|u\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}=\sup_{|y|<b,\ k\in\mathbb{Z}}|\hat{u}(y)_{k}|\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|k|}.

Recall that
\hat{u}(y)_{k}
is the
k
-th Fourier coefficient of
u(\cdot,y)
.
Since
|\hat{u}(y)_{k}|\leq|u|_{a,b}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi a|k|}
,

{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|u\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\leq|u|_{a,b}.

Also observe that for
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
a^{\prime}<a
,

\displaystyle|\left\langle{u}\right\rangle^{>N}|_{a^{\prime},b}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\geq N+1}\sup_{|y|<b}|\hat{u}(y)_{k}|\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a^{\prime}|k|}\leq\sum_{|k|\geq N+1}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|u\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(a-a^{\prime})|k|}

(9.74)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(a-a^{\prime})(N+1)}}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(a-a^{\prime})}}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|u\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\leq\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(a-a^{\prime})N}}{2\pi(a-a^{\prime})}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|u\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}.

In the last line, we used the elementary inequality
\mathrm{e}^{-x}(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x})^{-1}<x^{-1}
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
\epsilon>0
,
q\in\mathbb{Z}_{>1}
,
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq q}
,
5b<\alpha<a
and
5\epsilon<\beta<b
such that

qNb\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{q^{2}N\epsilon}{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(9.75)

Let
F
be a map of the form (
9.72
) with
|g|_{a,b}\leq\epsilon
.
Then there exists a near-identity analytic change of coordinates

\id+\Sigma:\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta}\to\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha/2}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta/2},

where
\Sigma(x,y)=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
for some
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}:\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta}\to\mathbb{C}
analytic and

|\sigma_{1}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}+|\sigma_{2}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}<q^{2}N{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b},

(9.76)

and the map
(\id+\Sigma)\circ F\circ(\id+\Sigma)^{-1}
in the new coordinates writes

(x,y)\mapsto(x+p/q+y,y+\widetilde{g}(x,y))

where
\widetilde{g}:\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta}\to\mathbb{C}
is real-analytic and

|\widetilde{g}-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}<\lambda{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\quad\text{and}\quad|\widetilde{g}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}<|g|_{a,b}+\lambda{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b},

(9.77)

where

\lambda=12\Big(\frac{q^{2}N\epsilon}{\beta}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\alpha N}}{\alpha}\Big).

Proof.

We shall construct
\id+\Sigma
as the inverse of the map
\Phi=\id+\Psi
where
\Psi(x,y)=(\psi(x,y),\varphi(x,y))
and
\varphi:=\psi\circ F-\psi
.
Let
p,q,a,b,\epsilon,N,\alpha,\beta
be given as in the statement.
Set the
k
-th Fourier coefficient of
\psi(\cdot,y)
to be

\hat{\psi}(y)_{k}=\frac{\hat{g}(y)_{k}}{2\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k(p/q+y)}[1-\cos(2\pi k(p/q+y))]}\qquad\text{($q\nmid k$ and $|k|\leq N$),}

and
\hat{\psi}(y)_{k}=0
for all other
k
.
By Fourier expansion, this choice of
\psi
is equivalent to

\psi\circ A^{2}-2\psi\circ A+\psi=-\{g\}_{q}^{N}.

Hence, by (
9.73
),

\widetilde{g}\circ\Phi=\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}+\{g\}_{q}^{>N}+(\psi\circ F^{2}-\psi\circ A^{2})+2(\psi\circ A-\psi\circ F).

Let us denote

\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=(\psi\circ F^{2}-\psi\circ A^{2})+2(\psi\circ A-\psi\circ F)

and

\mathcal{R}=\langle g\rangle_{q}^{>N}+\Big(\langle g\rangle_{q}\circ\Phi^{-1}-\langle g\rangle_{q}\Big)+\{g\}_{q}^{>N}\circ\Phi^{-1}+\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}

so that

\widetilde{g}=\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}+\mathcal{R}.

Step 1: estimate of
\psi
and
\varphi
.

By assumptions
Nb\leq 1/(2q)
and
q\geq 2
, for
|y|<b
,
|k|\leq N
and
q\nmid k
,

\bigg|1-\cos 2\pi k\Big(\frac{p}{q}+y\Big)\bigg|\geq 1-\cos\frac{\pi}{q}\geq\frac{4}{q^{2}}.

We have used the elementary inequality
1-\cos(2\pi x)\geq 16x^{2}
for
|x|\leq 1/4
.
Thus, by definition of
\psi
, we have

\displaystyle|\psi|_{a,b}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\nmid k,\\
|k|\leq N\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{2}}{8}\sup_{|y|<b}|\hat{g}(y)_{k}|\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\nmid k,\\
|k|\leq N\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{2}}{8}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\{g\}_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi|k|a}

(9.78)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle 2N\frac{q^{2}}{8}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\{g\}_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi a}<\frac{q^{2}N}{4}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\{g\}_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\leq\frac{q^{2}N}{4}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}.

Since
{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\leq|g|_{a,b}
, we have in particular that

|\psi|_{a,b}<\frac{q^{2}N\epsilon}{4}.

(9.79)

Since
|g|_{a,b}\leq\epsilon
, we also have
F(\mathbb{T}_{a-b+\epsilon}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\epsilon})\subset\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}
.
It follows from
\varphi=\psi\circ F-\psi
that

|\varphi|_{a-b+\epsilon,b-\epsilon}\leq 2|\psi|_{a,b}.

(9.80)

Step 2: estimate of
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
.

Let
a^{\prime}=a-\alpha/2
and
b^{\prime}=b-\beta/2
.
Using the explicit expression of
F
and the bound
|g|_{a,b}\leq\epsilon
as well as the assumptions
5\epsilon<\beta
and
5b<\alpha
, we deduce that the images of
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
under the maps
F^{2}
,
A^{2}
,
F
and
A
are all contained in
\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha/10}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta/10}
.
5
5

5

These images are actually contained in an even smaller domain, but we will not need this stronger fact.

By mean value theorem and Cauchy’s estimate,

\displaystyle|\psi\circ F^{2}-\psi\circ A^{2}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{|\Im x|<a^{\prime},\ |y|<b^{\prime}}\left|{\psi\Big(x+\frac{2p}{q}+2y+g(x,y),y+g(x,y)+g\circ F(x,y)\Big)-\psi\Big(x+\frac{2p}{q}+2y,y\Big)}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|g|_{a,b}\left|{\frac{\partial\psi}{\partial x}}\right|_{a-\alpha/10,b-\beta/10}+2|g|_{a,b}\left|{\frac{\partial\psi}{\partial y}}\right|_{a-\alpha/10,b-\beta/10}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{10}{\alpha}+\frac{20}{\beta}\Big)|\psi|_{a,b}|g|_{a,b}<\frac{22}{\beta}|\psi|_{a,b}|g|_{a,b}.

In the last inequality, we used
\alpha>5b>5\beta
.
Similarly,

|\psi\circ F-\psi\circ A|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}=|g|_{a,b}\left|{\frac{\partial\psi}{\partial y}}\right|_{a-\alpha/10,b-\beta/10}=\frac{10}{\beta}|\psi|_{a,b}|g|_{a,b}.

We have proved

|\mathcal{R}^{\prime}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq\frac{42}{\beta}|\psi|_{a,b}|g|_{a,b}.

Step 3: inverting
\Phi
.

Observe that since
|g|_{a,b}\leq\epsilon
, the function
\varphi=\psi\circ F-\psi
is well-defined on
\mathbb{T}_{a-b+\epsilon}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\epsilon}
and
|\varphi|_{a-b+\epsilon,b-\epsilon}\leq 2|\psi|_{a,b}
.
We shall apply Lemma
9.8
with
a_{0}=a-b+\epsilon
,
b_{0}=b-\epsilon
,
\delta_{1}=a_{0}-a^{\prime}
and
\delta_{2}=b_{0}-b^{\prime}
.
Since
a^{\prime}=a-\alpha/2
and
b^{\prime}=b-\beta/2
, we have
\delta_{1}>3\alpha/10
and
\delta_{2}>3\beta/10
using the assumptions
5\epsilon<\beta
and
5b<\alpha
.
By (
9.79
) and
\alpha>5\beta
, one verifies that the second assumption in (
9.75
) ensures the condition (
9.82
).
It now follows from Lemma
9.8
that
\Phi
is a diffeomorphism from
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
onto the image, which contains
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}-\delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}-\delta_{2}}
.
In particular, we may define

\Phi^{-1}=\id+\Sigma:\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta}\to\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}

where
\Sigma(x,y)=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
with

|\sigma_{1}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}\leq|\psi|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}};\qquad|\sigma_{2}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}\leq|\varphi|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}.

(9.81)

Combining with (
9.78
) and (
9.80
), we obtain (
9.76
).
Since we have shown that
\widetilde{g}\circ\Phi
is well-defined on
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
, by precomposing with
(\id+\Sigma)
, we deduce that
\widetilde{g}
is well-defined on
\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta}
as desired.

Step 4: estimate of
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{T}_{a-\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{b-\beta}
.

Similar to step 2, using mean value theorem and Cauchy’s estimate as well as (
9.81
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}\circ\Phi^{-1}-\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}}\right|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}\leq

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\partial\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}}{\partial x}}\right|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}|\sigma_{1}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}+\left|{\frac{\partial\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}}{\partial y}}\right|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}|\sigma_{2}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{2|\psi|_{a,b}}{\alpha}+\frac{4|\psi|_{a,b}}{\beta}\Big)|\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}|_{a,b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{22}{5\beta}|\psi|_{a,b}|g|_{a,b}.

On the other hand, applying (
9.74
) to
u=\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}
and
u=\{g\}_{q}
,

\displaystyle|\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{>N}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\alpha N}}{2\pi\alpha}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{>N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}

\displaystyle|\{g\}_{q}^{>N}\circ\Phi^{-1}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}\leq

\displaystyle|\{g\}_{q}^{>N}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\alpha N}}{\pi\alpha}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\{g\}_{q}^{>N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}.

Lastly, we have the obvious bound
|\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}\leq|\mathcal{R}^{\prime}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}

Thus,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}|_{a-\alpha,b-\beta}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi\alpha N}}{2\pi\alpha}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{>N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\alpha N}}{\pi\alpha}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\{g\}_{q}^{>N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}+\frac{22}{5\beta}|\psi|_{a,b}|g|_{a,b}+\frac{42}{\beta}|\psi|_{a,b}|g|_{a,b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg[\Big(\frac{1}{\pi}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\alpha N}}{2\pi}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\alpha N}}{\alpha}+\Big(\frac{22}{5}+42\Big)\frac{q^{2}N\epsilon}{4\beta}\bigg]{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle 12\Big(\frac{q^{2}N\epsilon}{\beta}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\alpha N}}{\alpha}\Big){\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}.

We have proven the first line of (
9.77
).
To prove the second line of (
9.77
), it is enough to apply
|\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}|_{a,b}\leq|g|_{a,b}
and the preceding estimates to

\widetilde{g}=\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}+\Big(\langle g\rangle_{q}\circ\Phi^{-1}-\langle g\rangle_{q}\Big)+\{g\}_{q}^{>N}\circ\Phi^{-1}+\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}.

∎

Lemma 9.8
.

Suppose
\psi,\varphi:\mathbb{T}_{a_{0}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{0}}\to\mathbb{C}
are analytic and, for some
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
,

\frac{|\psi|_{a_{0},b_{0}}}{\delta_{1}}+\frac{|\varphi|_{a_{0},b_{0}}}{\delta_{2}}<1.

(9.82)

Then the map
\Phi=\id+\Psi
with
\Psi(x,y)=(\psi(x,y),\varphi(x,y))
is an analytic diffeomorphism from
\mathbb{T}_{a_{0}-\delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{0}-\delta_{2}}
onto its image and surjects onto
\mathbb{T}_{a_{0}-2\delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{0}-2\delta_{2}}
.
The inverse
\id+\Sigma:\mathbb{T}_{a_{0}-2\delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{0}-2\delta_{2}}\to\mathbb{T}_{a_{0}-\delta_{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{0}-\delta_{2}}
of
\Phi
satisfies
\Sigma(x,y)=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
and

|\sigma_{1}|_{a_{0}-2\delta_{1}}\leq|\psi|_{a_{0}-\delta_{1}};\quad|\sigma_{2}|_{a_{0}-2\delta_{1}}\leq|\varphi|_{a_{0}-\delta_{1}}

9.2.2
Proof of Proposition
9.6

Proof.

By Proposition
9.1
, there exists a universal constant
c
such that, for sufficiently large
q
, we have

|\widetilde{g}_{q}|_{1-1/q,|\omega-p/q|/2}\leq c(1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M})\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

Let us pick an integer
n\in(2,\nu-3)

and define

\epsilon=c\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2};\quad\mathfrak{N}=q^{n};\quad\alpha=\frac{1}{q^{2}};\quad\beta=\frac{1}{6q}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|;\quad b=\frac{1}{2}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|.

By the choice of
n
, there exists
q_{0}\gg 1
such that for all
q\geq q_{0}
satisfying (
9.67
),

\displaystyle\frac{b}{\alpha}=\frac{q^{2}}{2}|\omega-p/q|\leq\frac{q^{2-\nu}}{2}<\frac{1}{5};

(9.83)

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{\beta}=6cq\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|\leq 6cq^{1-\nu}<\frac{1}{5};

\displaystyle qNb=\frac{1}{2}q^{n+1}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}q^{n+1-\nu}\leq\frac{1}{2};

\displaystyle\frac{q^{2}N\epsilon}{\beta}=\frac{6cq^{3+n}}{|\omega-p/q|}\leq 6cq^{3+n-\nu}<\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}}{24};

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi\alpha\mathfrak{N}}=q^{2}\mathrm{e}^{-\pi q^{n-2}}<\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}}{24};

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi q^{n-1}}\leq q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\quad\text{and}\quad q\geq\kappa.

Fix such a
q
and define

a=1-\frac{1}{q};\qquad a^{\prime}=1-\frac{2}{q};\qquad b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{3}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|.

The first five lines of (
9.83
) ensure that for any
\tilde{a}\in(a^{\prime}+\alpha,a)
and
\tilde{b}\in(b^{\prime}+\beta,b)
, Lemma
9.7
applies to any map
F
of the form (
9.72
) with analytic
g:\mathbb{T}_{\tilde{a}}\times\mathbb{D}_{\tilde{b}}\to\mathbb{C}
such that
|g|_{\tilde{a},\tilde{b}}\leq\epsilon
.
The lemma produces a coordinate transform
\id+\Sigma
such that the function
\widetilde{g}
in the new coordinates satisfies

\displaystyle|\widetilde{g}-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}|_{\tilde{a}-\alpha,\tilde{b}-\beta}<

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{\tilde{a},\tilde{b}}

\displaystyle|\widetilde{g}|_{\tilde{a}-\alpha,\tilde{b}-\beta}<

\displaystyle|g|_{\tilde{a},\tilde{b}}+\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{\tilde{a},\tilde{b}}.

For notational convenience, we define

\mathcal{T}g:=\widetilde{g};\qquad\mathcal{R}g:=\widetilde{g}-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}

so that the preceding inequalities write

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}g|_{\tilde{a}-\alpha,\tilde{b}-\beta}<

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{\tilde{a},\tilde{b}}

(9.84)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}g|_{\tilde{a}-\alpha,\tilde{b}-\beta}<

\displaystyle|g|_{\tilde{a},\tilde{b}}+\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{\tilde{a},\tilde{b}}.

Let us also put
a_{j}=a-j\alpha
and
b_{j}=b-j\beta
.
Then
a_{q}=a^{\prime}
and
b_{q}=b^{\prime}
.
In order to iterate Lemma
9.7
multiple times starting from an analytic
g:\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}\to\mathbb{C}
, we suppose

|g|_{a,b}\leq(1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M})\epsilon.

(9.85)

Then we show by induction on
1\leq j\leq q
that, after repeatedly applying Lemma
9.7
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{j}g=\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}+\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}\left\langle{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{k-1}g}\right\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}+\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g,

(9.86)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g|_{a_{j},b_{j}}<\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mj}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\quad\text{and}\quad|\mathcal{T}^{j}g|_{a_{j},b_{j}}<\sum_{k=0}^{j}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mk}|g|_{a,b}.

(9.87)

The case of
j=1
follows from definitions of
\mathcal{T}g
and
\mathcal{R}g
as well as (
9.84
).
Let us assume (
9.86
) and (
9.87
) hold for some
1\leq j<q
.
Then, the assumption (
9.85
) ensures that
|\mathcal{T}^{j}g|_{a_{j},b_{j}}<(1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M})^{-1}|g|_{a,b}\leq\epsilon
and hence Lemma
9.7
applies to
\mathcal{T}^{j}g
.
Notice that
\mathcal{T}^{j}g-\langle\mathcal{T}^{j}g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}=\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g-\langle\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}
.
Therefore, by (
9.84
) with
g
replaced by
\mathcal{T}^{j}g
and the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j}g|_{a_{j+1},b_{j+1}}<

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\mathcal{T}^{j}g-\langle\mathcal{T}^{j}g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a_{j},b_{j}}

(9.88)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g-\langle\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a_{j},b_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g|_{a_{j},b_{j}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M(j+1)}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}

Thus, we obtain the first inequality of (
9.87
) for the case
j+1
.
To obtain the second inequality of (
9.87
), we apply the second inequality of (
9.84
) with
g
replaced by
\mathcal{T}^{j}g
and use the induction hypothesis and (
9.88
) to obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}^{j+1}g|_{a_{j+1},b_{j+1}}<

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}^{j}g|_{a_{j},b_{j}}+\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\mathcal{T}^{j}g-\langle\mathcal{T}^{j}g\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a_{j},b_{j}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{j}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mk}|g|_{a,b}+\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M(j+1)}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g-\langle g\rangle_{q}^{N}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{j+1}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mk}|g|_{a,b}.

The induction is finished.
Let us now take
g=\widetilde{g}_{q}
for the analytic function
\widetilde{g}_{q}
obtained from Proposition
9.1
and take
j=q
and
g_{q}:=\mathcal{T}^{q}\widetilde{g}_{q}
.
We readily get

\displaystyle|\{g_{q}\}_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle|\{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{q-1}\widetilde{g}_{q}\}_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq 2|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{q-1}\widetilde{g}_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}<2\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mq}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mq}|\widetilde{g}_{q}|_{a,b}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mq}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}

and

\displaystyle|g_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}<\frac{|\widetilde{g}_{q}|_{a,b}}{(1-\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M})}\lesssim\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

We have proved (
9.68
) and (
9.69
).
We now show (
9.70
).
Since
N=\kappa q\leq\mathfrak{N}
by the last inequality in (
9.83
), Equation (
9.86
) implies

\langle g_{q}\rangle_{q}^{N}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{N}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\langle\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{N}.

By the first inequality in (
9.87
), we have
|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}\widetilde{g}_{q}|_{a_{j},b_{j}}<\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mj}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}
.
Thus, fixing
y=0
and substituting the definition of
a_{j}
, we find

\displaystyle\|\langle\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}\widetilde{g}_{q}(\cdot,0)\rangle_{q}^{N}\|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq N,\ q\mid k}|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-1}\widetilde{g}_{q}|_{a_{j},b_{j}}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi a_{j}|k|}\mathrm{e}^{2\pi|k|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq N,\ q\mid k}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mj}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\mathrm{e}^{2\pi(1/q+j/q^{2})|k|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{2N}{q}+1\Big)\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mj}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\frac{2N}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2\kappa+1)\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mj}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\mathrm{e}^{4\pi\kappa}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\mathrm{e}^{4\pi\kappa}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mj}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}.

Summing over
1\leq j\leq q
and using
M\geq 1
, we obtain

\|\langle g_{q}(\cdot,0)\rangle_{q}^{N}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}(\cdot,0)\rangle_{q}^{N}\|\lesssim\kappa\mathrm{e}^{4\pi\kappa}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}.

(9.89)

On the other hand, we may write

\widetilde{g}_{q}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}=\{\widetilde{g}_{q}\}_{q}+\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}+h\rangle_{q}^{>\mathfrak{N}}-\langle h\rangle^{>\mathfrak{N}},

where

h(x,y)=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega+y\Big)\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega-y\Big)\frac{\langle\dot{f}_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}.

Then, by the first line in (
9.10
),
we have

{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\{\widetilde{g}_{q}\}_{q}+\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}+h\rangle_{q}^{>\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\leq|\widetilde{g}_{q}+h|_{a,b}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{3}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}

and, since the
\|\cdot\|
-norm of
\langle\dot{f}_{1}\rangle_{q}(1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q})^{-1}
is bounded, by the choice of
\mathfrak{N}
and the last line of (
9.83
),

{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\langle h\rangle^{>\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(1-a)\mathfrak{N}}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\langle h\rangle^{>\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{1,b}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi q^{n-1}}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}-\widetilde{g}_{q}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

(9.90)

Combining with (
9.89
) we obtain (
9.70
).

It now remains to show (
9.71
).
Let us denote by
\id+\Sigma_{j}:\mathbb{T}_{a_{j}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{j}}\to\mathbb{T}_{a_{j-1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{j-1}}
the coordinate transform obtained from the
j
-th iteration of the Lemma.
Let us write
\Sigma_{j}(x,y)=(\sigma_{1,j}(x,y),\sigma_{2,j}(x,y))
for some analytic
\sigma_{1,j},\sigma_{2,j}:\mathbb{T}_{a_{j}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b_{j}}\to\mathbb{C}
.
By (
9.76
), (
9.86
), (
9.87
) and (
9.90
),

\displaystyle|\sigma_{1,j}|_{a_{j},b_{j}}+|\sigma_{2,j}|_{a_{j},b_{j}}<

\displaystyle q^{2}\mathfrak{N}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g_{q}-\langle\mathcal{T}^{j-1}g_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a_{j-1},b_{j-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle q^{2}\mathfrak{N}|\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}^{j-2}g_{q}|_{a_{j-1},b_{j-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle q^{2}\mathfrak{N}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M(j-1)}{\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|\kern-0.6458pt\left|g_{q}-\langle g_{q}\rangle_{q}^{\mathfrak{N}}\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|\kern-0.6458pt\right|}_{a,b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M(j-1)}q^{2+n+\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

On the other hand, by composing
(\id+\Sigma):=(\id+\Sigma_{1})\circ(\id+\Sigma_{2})\circ\cdots\circ(\id+\Sigma_{q})
, we find that

\Sigma=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\Sigma_{j}\circ(\id+\Sigma_{j+1})\circ(\id+\Sigma_{j+2})\circ\cdots\circ(\id+\Sigma_{q}).

Therefore, writing
\Sigma(x,y)=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
, we have

|\sigma_{1}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}+|\sigma_{2}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{q}|\sigma_{1,j}|_{a_{j},b_{j}}+|\sigma_{2,j}|_{a_{j},b_{j}}<\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi M(j-1)}q^{2+n+\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

For concreteness, we may fix
n=3
.
Then the proof of (
9.71
) is finished by summing the geometric series in
j
.
∎

9.3
Step 3: control
g^{*}_{q}
using exactness and the end of the proof

We are now in position to prove Theorem
6.1
.
So far, we have essentially obtained all the required results in the statement of Theorem
6.1
with the exception of part (3).
The proof of part (3) will be the main focus of this section.
The main idea for the proof of part (3) is to use the exactness property of the original standard map and the fact that the resonant coordinates do not ‘distort’ the standard area form by much.
Then, using an elementary geometric property of exact symplectic map, we can make use of the estimate of the zero-average part
g^{\bullet}_{q}
to estimate the average part
g^{*}_{q}
.
We remark that a similar idea was also used in
[
13
]
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Fix
\omega\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Q}
and
\phi:\mathbb{T}_{1}\to\mathbb{C}
analytic with
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
.
Let
\kappa\geq 2
and
\nu>\nu_{0}:=64
be given as in the statement of Theorem
6.1
.
Consider
q\gg 1
such that the arithmetic condition (
6.1
) holds and let
p
be the corresponding minimizer of
|q\omega-p|
over
p\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Since the condition (
6.1
) is stronger than the arithmetic condition (
9.7
) of Proposition
9.1
, taking
q
sufficiently large ensures that Proposition
9.1
applies and we accordingly obtain a coordinate transform
\Theta
and a map

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{q}=\Theta^{-1}\circ F\circ\Theta:\mathbb{T}_{1-1/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2}\to

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{1}\times\mathbb{C}

\displaystyle(x,y)\mapsto

\displaystyle\Big(x+\frac{p}{q}+y,\ y+\widetilde{g}_{q}(x,y)\Big)

satisfying the conclusions of Proposition
9.1
.

Increasing
q
if necessary, we may then apply Proposition
9.6
with
M=\kappa+1
(and
\nu
,
\kappa
as given in the theorem statement) to obtain a second coordinate transform
\id+\Sigma
and a map

\displaystyle F_{q}=(\id+\Sigma)^{-1}\circ\widetilde{F}_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma):\mathbb{T}_{1-2/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/3}\to

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{1}\times\mathbb{C}

\displaystyle(x,y)\mapsto

\displaystyle\Big(x+\frac{p}{q}+y,\ y+g_{q}(x,y)\Big)

satisfying the conclusions of Proposition
9.6
.
We now prove that the change of coordinates
H_{q}:=\Theta\circ(\id+\Sigma)
, the map
F_{q}
and the function
g_{q}
satisfy the conclusions of Theorem
6.1
.

Let
a=1-3/q
and
b=|\omega-p/q|/5
as in the theorem statement.

Let
h_{q,1}
and
h_{q,2}
be defined as in (
6.2
). By (
9.8
) and
\Sigma(x,y)=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
, we have

h_{q,1}=\sigma_{1}+\theta_{1}\circ(\id+\Sigma),\qquad h_{q,2}=\sigma_{2}+\theta_{2}\circ(\id+\Sigma).

Since
(\id+\Sigma)(\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b})\subset\mathbb{T}_{1-1/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/2}
, it follows from (
9.9
) and (
9.71
) as well as the choice
\nu\geq\nu_{0}+6
that

|h_{q,1}|_{a,b}\lesssim q\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|;\qquad|h_{q,2}|_{a,b}<\frac{1}{10}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|

for
q
sufficiently large.
We have obtained (
6.3
).

Next, from the conclusion (
9.68
) of Proposition
9.6
we have immediately (
6.5
).
Next, to obtain (
6.6
), we use (
9.68
), (
9.69
) and the choices
N=\kappa q
,
M=\kappa/2
to get

\displaystyle|g_{q}-\langle g_{q}\rangle_{q}^{N}|_{1/q,b}\leq

\displaystyle|\{g_{q}\}_{q}|_{1/q,b}+|\langle g_{q}\rangle_{q}^{>N}|_{1/q,b}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\{g_{q}\}_{q}|_{1-2/q,|\omega-p/q|/3}+\sum_{|j|>N,\ q\mid j}|g_{q}|_{1-2/q,|\omega-p/q|/3}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(1-2/q-1/q)|j|}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mq}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}+\sum_{|j|>N,\ q\mid j}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(1-3/q)|j|}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi Mq}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}+\mathrm{e}^{6\pi\kappa}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(\kappa+1)q}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{6\pi\kappa}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(\kappa+1)q}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

We have proven (
6.6
).

We shall postpone the proof of (
6.7
) to the end of this section.
To prove (
6.9
), we denote

\mathcal{Q}(x,y)=\widetilde{g}_{q}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega+y\Big)\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega-y\Big)\frac{\langle\dot{f}_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}(x)}.

Then, by the first inequality of (
9.10
), we have
|\mathcal{Q}|_{1-1/q,|\omega-p/q|/2}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}|\omega-p/q|^{3}
.
It follows from the definition of the
\|\cdot\|
-norm and the choice
N=\kappa q
that

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle{\mathcal{Q}(\cdot,0)}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid k\\
|k|\leq N\end{subarray}}|\hat{\mathcal{Q}}(0)_{k}|\mathrm{e}^{2\pi|k|}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid k\\
|k|\leq N\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{Q}|_{1-1/q,|\omega-p/q|/2}\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi(1-1/q)|k|}e^{2\pi|k|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{2N}{q}+1\Big)|\mathcal{Q}|_{1-1/q,|\omega-p/q|/2}\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\frac{N}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\kappa}q^{\nu_{0}}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{3}.

Combining with (
9.70
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle{g_{q}(\cdot,0)+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{p}{q}-\omega\Big)^{2}\frac{\langle\dot{f}_{1}\rangle_{q}}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}}}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\langle g_{q}(\cdot,0)\rangle_{q}^{N}-\langle\widetilde{g}_{q}(\cdot,0)\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|+\left\|\left\langle{\mathcal{Q}(\cdot,0)}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\mathrm{e}^{4\pi\kappa}q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}+\kappa\mathrm{e}^{2\pi\kappa}q^{\nu_{0}}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\mathrm{e}^{4\pi\kappa}q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{2}.

We have proven (
6.9
).
To prove (
6.10
),
we first note that, by combining the second inequality of (
9.10
) with the estimate
\|f_{1}\|<1/18
from (
9.36
), we have
|\widetilde{S}_{q}|_{1-1/q,|\omega-p/q|/2}\lesssim\left|{\omega-p/q}\right|^{2}
.
Since
S_{q}=S\circ H_{q}=\widetilde{S}_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma)
, by (
9.71
) and Cauchy’s estimate we have

\displaystyle|S_{q}-\widetilde{S}_{q}|_{a,b}\leq

\displaystyle|\sigma_{2}|_{a,b}\left|{\frac{\partial\widetilde{S}_{q}}{\partial y}}\right|_{1-2/q,|\omega-p/q|/3}+|\sigma_{1}|_{a,b}\left|{\frac{\partial\widetilde{S}_{q}}{\partial x}}\right|_{1-2/q,|\omega-p/q|/3}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle q|\sigma_{2}|_{a,b}\left|{\widetilde{S}_{q}}\right|_{1-1/q,|\omega-p/q|/2}+\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{-1}|\sigma_{1}|_{a,b}\left|{\widetilde{S}_{q}}\right|_{1-1/q,|\omega-p/q|/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle q^{5+\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|^{3}.

We have assumed
q
is sufficiently large so that the map
\id+\Sigma
sends
\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}
into
\mathbb{T}_{1-2/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/3}
.
Since
\nu>5
, the conclusion (
6.10
) follows from the above estimate and the second line of (
9.10
) by a triangle inequality.

Finally, it remains to prove (
6.7
).
Informally, the claim states that
g_{q}(\cdot,0)
is dominated by its zero average (oscillating) part
g_{q}^{\bullet}
.
This is expected from the fact that the map
F_{q}
preserves an area form close to the standard form
\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y
:
if the preserved area form were exactly
\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y
, then
g_{q}^{*}
would be identically zero, whereas for an area form close to
\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y
, the average part
g_{q}^{*}
is expected to be small compared to the oscillatory part
g_{q}^{\bullet}
.

We make this argument precise as follows.
Observe that, if
\phi
is real-analytic, then the map
F_{q}
is real-analytic and exact symplectic with the (nonstandard) symplectic 1-form
H^{*}_{q}(y\mathrm{d}x)
, whose exterior derivative is an area form
H^{*}_{q}(\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y)
preserved by
F_{q}
.
By (
9.11
), the area form
H^{*}_{q}(\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y)
writes

\displaystyle(\Theta\circ(\id+\Sigma))^{*}(\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\id+\Sigma)^{*}\Big(\Big(\frac{1}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}}+\widetilde{\eta}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma)}+\widetilde{\eta}\circ(\id+\Sigma)\Big)\Big(1+\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}+\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial x}\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}-\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial y}\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial x}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

where
\widetilde{\eta}
,
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
are analytic functions satisfying (
9.12
) and (
9.71
) respectively.
This expression holds on the domain
\mathbb{T}_{1-2/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/3}
.
In particular,

|\widetilde{\eta}\circ(\id+\Sigma)|_{1-2/q,|\omega-p/q|/3}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|.

By Cauchy’s estimate and (
9.71
),

\left|{\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}+\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial x}\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial y}-\frac{\partial\sigma_{1}}{\partial y}\frac{\partial\sigma_{2}}{\partial x}}\right|_{1-3/q,|\omega-p/q|/4}\lesssim q^{5+\nu_{0}-\nu}\left|{\omega-\frac{p}{q}}\right|.

Since
\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}=f_{1}^{*}+\langle f_{1}^{\bullet}\rangle_{q}
, we may write

\frac{1}{1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma)}=\frac{1}{1+f_{1}^{*}}-\frac{\langle f_{1}^{\bullet}\rangle_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma)}{(1+f_{1}^{*})(1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma))}.

Since the smallest order of nonzero Fourier coefficients of
\langle f_{1}^{\bullet}\rangle_{q}
is at least
q
, using
\|f_{1}\|<1/18
from (
9.36
) and the smallness of
\sigma_{1}
, the quantity
\langle f_{1}^{\bullet}\rangle_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma)(x,y)
for
\Im x=0
can be bounded by
\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi q}
.
The second term on the right-hand side in the above equation can then be estimated as

\left|{\frac{\langle f_{1}^{\bullet}\rangle_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma)}{(1+f_{1}^{*})(1+\langle f_{1}\rangle_{q}\circ(\id+\Sigma))}}\right|_{0,|\omega-p/q|/4}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi q}.

Here and henceforth, we denote by
\left|{\;\cdot\;}\right|_{0,b}
the supremum norm over the domain
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}
.
Combining the above estimates and using
\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi q}\ll q^{-\nu}
and
|\omega-p/q|\leq q^{-\nu}
, we find that

H_{q}^{*}(\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y)=\Big(\frac{1}{1+f_{1}^{*}}+\eta\Big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y,\qquad\text{with}\qquad|\eta|_{0,|\omega-p/q|/4}\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}.

(9.91)

By construction, the function
\eta:\mathbb{T}_{1-3/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/4}\to\mathbb{C}
depends analytically on
\phi
.

If
\phi
is real-analytic, then the changes of coordinates are all real-analytic and therefore
\eta
is real-analytic.
By the standard property of exact symplectic maps, the
signed
area of the region bounded by the curve
\mathbb{T}\times\{y\}
and its image under
F_{q}
is zero for a fixed
real

y
, i.e.,

\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{y}^{y+g_{q}(x,y)}\frac{1}{1+f_{1}^{*}}+\eta(x,y)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x=0.

(9.92)

Since
\eta
is analytic on
\mathbb{T}_{1-3/q}\times\mathbb{D}_{|\omega-p/q|/4}
and
|g_{q}|_{1-3/q,|\omega-p/q|/4}\ll|\omega-p/q|
by (
6.5
), the left-hand side of the equation above is well-defined and analytic for
y
in the complex disc
\mathbb{D}_{b}
with
b=|\omega-p/q|/5
.

Fix
y\in\mathbb{D}_{b}\cap\mathbb{R}
. Consider now the Fourier expansion of
\phi
of the following form

\phi(x)=\sum_{k\geq 1}a_{k}\sin(2\pi kx)+b_{k}\cos(2\pi kx)

where
a_{k},b_{k}\in\mathbb{C}
.
If
a_{k},b_{k}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
k
, then
\phi
is real-analytic and the equation (
9.92
) holds.
Suppose
a_{k}=b_{k}=0
for all but finitely many
k
’s.
Then we may regard the left hand side of (
9.92
) as an analytic function in the finitely many variables
a_{k},b_{k}
.
Since the equation (
9.92
) holds for all real values of
a_{k},b_{k}
, it must hold for all complex values of
a_{k},b_{k}
by analytic continuation.
By approximating a general
\phi
by its Fourier truncations and using the continuous dependence of
\eta
and
g_{q}
on
\phi
, we conclude that (
9.92
) holds for all analytic
\phi:\mathbb{T}_{1}\to\mathbb{C}
such that
\|\dddot{\phi}\|\leq 1
.

Applying the above argument for each fixed real
y
and using analytic continuation again, we deduce that (
9.92
) holds for all
y\in\mathbb{D}_{b}
.

We now use (
9.92
) to control
g_{q}^{*}(y)
.
Fixing
|y|<b
and rewriting the left hand side of (
9.92
), we have

\frac{g^{*}_{q}(y)}{1+f_{1}^{*}}+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{0}^{1}g_{q}(x,y)\eta(x,y+tg_{q}(x,y))\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x=0

The second term on the left hand side can be bounded as

\left|{\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{0}^{1}g_{q}(x,y)\eta(x,y+tg_{q}(x,y))\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}\right|\leq|\eta|_{0,|\omega-p/q|/4}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}|g_{q}(x,y)|

Therefore, we obtain from (
9.91
) that

|g_{q}^{*}(y)|\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}|g_{q}(x,y)|.

Note that
g_{q}(x,y)=g_{q}^{*}(y)+g_{q}^{\bullet}(x,y)
.
Since
\nu_{0}<\nu
, for
q
sufficiently the quantity
q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}
is arbitrarily small. Hence we may write

|g_{q}^{*}(y)|\lesssim q^{\nu_{0}-\nu}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}|g_{q}^{\bullet}(x,y)|.

We have obtained (
6.7
) and hence completed the proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix A
Functions defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}

Recall that for
\lambda>0
we have defined the domain
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
to be

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}_{1}\times\mathbb{C}\mid|\Im x|+\lambda^{-1}|y|<1\}.

(A.1)

If
u
is a function defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, we denote by
|u|^{(\lambda)}
the supremum norm of
u
over
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.
If
u
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{r}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, we set

|u|^{(\lambda,r)}:=\sup\bigg\{\biggl|\frac{\partial^{j+k}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k}}(x,y)\biggr|\ :\ (x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda},\ 0\leq j+k\leq r\bigg\}.

Lemma A.1

(Cauchy’s estimate)
.

Let
\lambda>0
and
\bar{u}:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\to\mathbb{C}
be analytic, and put
u(x,y)=y\,\bar{u}(x,y)
.
Then, for any
r\geq 1
and any
\lambda^{\prime}\in(0,\lambda)
,

|u|^{(\lambda^{\prime},r)}\;\leq\;\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\lambda^{\prime}/\lambda}\;|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda,\,r-1)}.

(A.2)

In particular,

\biggl|\frac{\partial u}{\partial x}\biggr|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}\leq\frac{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)},\qquad\biggl|\frac{\partial u}{\partial y}\biggr|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}\leq\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)}.

(A.3)

Proof.

Throughout the proof, let us fix
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.

Step 1: first derivatives.

By (
A.1
), for fixed
y
the available
x
–strip in
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
has half-width
1-|y|/\lambda
and in
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
has half-width
1-|y|/\lambda^{\prime}
. Cauchy’s estimate in
x
gives

\displaystyle\biggl|y\,\frac{\partial\bar{u}}{\partial x}(x,y)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\;|y|\Bigl(\Bigl(1-\frac{|y|}{\lambda}\Bigr)-\Bigl(1-\frac{|y|}{\lambda^{\prime}}\Bigr)\Bigr)^{-1}|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)}\;\leq\frac{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)}.

(A.4)

For fixed
x
, the available
y
–radii are
\lambda(1-|\Im x|)
in
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and
\lambda^{\prime}(1-|\Im x|)
in
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
. Cauchy’s estimate in
y
yields

\displaystyle\biggl|y\,\frac{\partial\bar{u}}{\partial y}(x,y)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{|y|}{\lambda(1-|\Im x|)-\lambda^{\prime}(1-|\Im x|)}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)}=\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)}.

Since
\partial_{y}u=\bar{u}+y\,\partial_{y}\bar{u}
, we obtain

\biggl|\frac{\partial u}{\partial y}\biggr|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}\leq|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)}\leq\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda)},

and together with (
A.4
) this proves (
A.3
).

Step 2: higher derivatives.

Let
1\leq j+k\leq r
. Applying the previous estimates to

\partial_{x}^{\,j-1}\partial_{y}^{\,k}\bar{u}
(if
k=0
) or to

\partial_{x}^{\,j}\partial_{y}^{\,k-1}\bar{u}
(if
k\geq 1
) gives

\biggl|y\,\frac{\partial^{j+k}\bar{u}}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k}}(x,y)\biggr|\leq\begin{cases}\dfrac{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\;\Bigl|\dfrac{\partial^{j-1}\bar{u}}{\partial x^{j-1}}\Bigr|^{(\lambda)}&\text{if }k=0,\\
\dfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\;\Bigl|\dfrac{\partial^{j+k-1}\bar{u}}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k-1}}\Bigr|^{(\lambda)}&\text{if }k\geq 1.\end{cases}

By the Leibniz rule,

\biggl|\frac{\partial^{j+k}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k}}(x,y)\biggr|=\biggl|y\,\frac{\partial^{j+k}\bar{u}}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k}}(x,y)+k\,\frac{\partial^{j+k-1}\bar{u}}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k-1}}(x,y)\biggr|\leq\max\!\Bigl\{\frac{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}},\;\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}+r\Bigr\}\;|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda,\,r-1)}.

Since
\frac{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}=\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\lambda^{\prime}/\lambda}
and

\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}+r<\frac{1}{1-\lambda^{\prime}/\lambda}+r<\frac{r}{1-\lambda^{\prime}/\lambda}
, we get

\sup_{1\leq j+k\leq r}\biggl|\frac{\partial^{j+k}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial y^{k}}\biggr|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}<\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda}}\;|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda,\,r-1)}.

Step 3: zeroth order.

For
j=k=0
, using
|y|<\lambda^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
,

|u(x,y)|\leq|y|\,|\bar{u}(x,y)|\leq\lambda^{\prime}\,|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}\leq\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\frac{\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda}}\;|\bar{u}|^{(\lambda,\,r-1)}.

Taking the supremum over
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
and
0\leq j+k\leq r
yields (
A.2
).
∎

Proposition A.2

(Composition)
.

Let
\lambda>\lambda^{\prime}>0
and suppose
\bar{\sigma}_{j}:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}
is analytic for
j=1,2
.
Define
\sigma_{j}(x,y):=y\,\bar{\sigma}_{j}(x,y)
for
j=1,2
, set
\Sigma(x,y):=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
, and consider the map
\id+\Sigma:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}^{2}
.
Then:

1.

If

|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}+\frac{1+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}}{\lambda}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}},

(A.5)

then
\id+\Sigma
maps
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
into
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.

2.

For all
r\geq 0
, there exists a combinatorial constant
\tilde{C}_{r}>0
depending only on
r
such that,
whenever the composition
v\circ(\id+\Sigma)
is well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
for an analytic
v:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\to\mathbb{C}
,
