|v\circ(\id+\Sigma)|^{(\lambda^{\prime},r)}\;\leq\;\tilde{C}_{r}\,|v|^{(\lambda,r)}\,\bigl(1+|\sigma_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},r)}+|\sigma_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},r)}\bigr)^{r}.

(A.6)

3.

Let
v_{j}:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\to\mathbb{C}
be analytic for
j=1,2
such that
v_{j}\circ(\id+\Sigma)
is well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
,
and suppose
\bar{\sigma}_{j}
satisfies

\bar{\sigma}_{j}\;=\;v_{j}\circ(\id+\Sigma)\qquad(j=1,2).

(A.7)

Then, for any
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, there exist constants
A_{r},B_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
C_{r}>0
depending only on
r
such that, for all
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\lambda^{\prime})
,

|\bar{\sigma}_{j}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}\;\leq\;C_{r}\Bigl(\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\Bigr)^{A_{r}}\Bigl(1+|v_{1}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}+|v_{2}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}\Bigr)^{B_{r}}|v_{j}|^{(\lambda,r)},\qquad\beta:=\Bigl(\frac{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}{\lambda^{\prime}}\Bigr)^{\!1/r},

(A.8)

with the convention that
|v_{j}|^{(\lambda,-1)}:=0
.

Proof.

(1)
Let
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, so
|\Im x|<1-|y|/\lambda^{\prime}
.
Using
|\Im(x+\sigma_{1})|\leq|\Im x|+|\sigma_{1}|
and
|\sigma_{j}|\leq|y|\,|\bar{\sigma}_{j}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}
,

\displaystyle|\Im(x+\sigma_{1}(x,y))|+\frac{|y+\sigma_{2}(x,y)|}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\;|\Im x|+|y|\Bigl(|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}+\frac{1+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}}{\lambda}\Bigr)

\displaystyle<\;1-\frac{|y|}{\lambda^{\prime}}+|y|\Bigl(|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}+\frac{1+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime})}}{\lambda}\Bigr).

If (
A.5
) holds, then the term between the parentheses is
\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}
, and the right-hand side is
<1
.
Hence
(x+\sigma_{1}(x,y),y+\sigma_{2}(x,y))\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.

(2)
Differentiate
v(x+\sigma_{1}(x,y),\,y+\sigma_{2}(x,y))
up to total order
r
in
(x,y)
.
By the multivariate chain rule (Faà di Bruno), each derivative is a finite linear combination (with coefficients depending only on
r
) of terms

\frac{\partial^{\alpha+\beta}v}{\partial x^{\alpha}\partial y^{\beta}}\bigl(\varsigma_{1}(x,y),\varsigma_{2}(x,y)\bigr)\prod_{\ell=1}^{\alpha}\frac{\partial^{j_{\ell}+k_{\ell}}\varsigma_{1}}{\partial x^{j_{\ell}}\partial y^{k_{\ell}}}(x,y)\;\prod_{\ell=1}^{\beta}\frac{\partial^{j^{\prime}_{\ell}+k^{\prime}_{\ell}}\varsigma_{2}}{\partial x^{j^{\prime}_{\ell}}\partial y^{k^{\prime}_{\ell}}}(x,y),

where
\varsigma_{j}(x,y)=x+\sigma_{j}(x,y)
, and

\sum_{\ell=1}^{\alpha}(j_{\ell}+k_{\ell})+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\beta}(j^{\prime}_{\ell}+k^{\prime}_{\ell})\leq r
.
Since the composition is well-defined, we have
(\varsigma_{1}(x,y),\varsigma_{2}(x,y))\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and hence

\bigl|\partial^{\alpha+\beta}v\bigr|^{(\lambda)}\leq|v|^{(\lambda,r)}
.
On the other hand, each derivative of
\varsigma_{j}
is bounded by
1+|\sigma_{j}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},r)}
.
Taking suprema and absorbing the number of terms into
\tilde{C}_{r}
yields (
A.6
).

(3)
We argue by induction on
r\geq 0
.

Base case
r=0
.
From (
A.7
) we have
|\bar{\sigma}_{j}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime})}\leq|v_{j}|^{(\lambda)}
, so (
A.8
) holds with
C_{0}=1
and
A_{0}=B_{0}=0
.

Induction step.
Fix
r\geq 1
and
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\lambda^{\prime})
, and set
\beta=(\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\lambda^{\prime})^{1/r}\in(0,1)
.
Applying (
A.6
) with
v=v_{j}
and using Lemma
A.1
to estimate
\sigma_{j}=y\bar{\sigma}_{j}
on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
from
\bar{\sigma}_{j}
on the larger domain
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta}\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, we get

|\bar{\sigma}_{j}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}\;\leq\;\tilde{C}_{r}\,|v_{j}|^{(\lambda,r)}\,\Bigl(1+|\sigma_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}+|\sigma_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}\Bigr)^{r}\;\leq\;\tilde{C}_{r}\,|v_{j}|^{(\lambda,r)}\,\Bigl(\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime\prime}}{1-\beta}\Bigr)^{\!r}\,\Bigl(1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta,\,r-1)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta,\,r-1)}\Bigr)^{\!r}.

Note that
\lambda^{\prime\prime}<\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta\leq\lambda^{\prime}
and

((\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta)/\lambda^{\prime})^{1/(r-1)}=\beta
.
Thus the induction hypothesis at level
r-1
(applied with
\lambda^{\prime\prime}
replaced by
\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta
) yields

1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta,\,r-1)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta,\,r-1)}\;\leq\;C_{r-1}\,\Bigl(\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta}{1-\beta}\Bigr)^{\!A_{r-1}}\Bigl(1+|v_{1}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}+|v_{2}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}\Bigr)^{B_{r-1}+1}.

Combining and using
\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\beta<\lambda^{\prime}
gives

|\bar{\sigma}_{j}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}\;\leq\;\tilde{C}_{r}\,C_{r-1}^{\,r}\,\Bigl(\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\Bigr)^{\,r+rA_{r-1}}\,\Bigl(1+|v_{1}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}+|v_{2}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}\Bigr)^{\,r(B_{r-1}+1)}|v_{j}|^{(\lambda,r)}.

Thus (
A.8
) holds with the recursive choices

A_{r}:=r+rA_{r-1},\qquad B_{r}:=r(B_{r-1}+1),\qquad C_{r}:=\tilde{C}_{r}\,C_{r-1}^{\,r},

and the stated base values.
∎

Remark

(Explicit choice of
\tilde{C}_{r}
in (
A.6
))
.

One may take

\tilde{C}_{0}:=1,\qquad\tilde{C}_{r}:=r!\,2^{\,r}\quad(r\geq 1).

This follows from the multivariate Faà di Bruno expansion for
v\circ(\id+\Sigma)
:
an
r
-th
(x,y)
–derivative is a sum of at most
r!\,2^{r}
terms, each bounded by

|v|^{(\lambda,r)}(1+|\sigma_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},r)}+|\sigma_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime},r)})^{r}
.

Proposition A.3

(Inversion)
.

Let
\bar{\xi}_{1},\bar{\xi}_{2}:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\to\mathbb{C}
be analytic and define
\xi_{j}(x,y):=y\,\bar{\xi}_{j}(x,y)
and
\Xi(x,y):=(\xi_{1}(x,y),\xi_{2}(x,y))
.
Suppose

0<\lambda^{\prime}<\frac{\bigl(1-|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda)}\bigr)^{2}}{\;\frac{1}{\lambda}+|\bar{\xi}_{1}|^{(\lambda)}\bigl(2-|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda)}\bigr)}.

(A.9)

Then there exist analytic functions
\bar{\sigma}_{1},\bar{\sigma}_{2}:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}
such that, with
\sigma_{j}(x,y):=y\,\bar{\sigma}_{j}(x,y)
and
\Sigma(x,y):=(\sigma_{1}(x,y),\sigma_{2}(x,y))
, we have

(\id+\Xi)\circ(\id+\Sigma)=\id\quad\text{on }\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}

(A.10)

and, for any
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\lambda^{\prime})
such that
(\id+\Xi)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
,

(\id+\Sigma)\circ(\id+\Xi)=\id\quad\text{on }\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}.

(A.11)

Moreover, for any
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
there exist constants
A_{r},B_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
C_{r}>0
(depending only on
r
) such that, for any
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\lambda^{\prime})
,

1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}\;\leq\;C_{r}\Bigl(\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\Bigr)^{A_{r}}\Biggl(\frac{1+|\bar{\xi}_{1}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}+|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}}{1-|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda)}}\Biggr)^{B_{r}}\Bigl(1+|\bar{\xi}_{1}|^{(\lambda,r)}+|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda,r)}\Bigr),

(A.12)

where
\beta:=(\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\lambda^{\prime})^{1/r}
.

Proof.

Set
a:=|\bar{\xi}_{1}|^{(\lambda)}
and
b:=|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda)}
(so
b<1
is implicit in (
A.9
)). From (
A.9
) one checks there exists
\lambda_{*}>0
such that

\lambda^{\prime}\;\leq\;\frac{1-b}{\frac{1}{\lambda_{*}}+a}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lambda_{*}\;<\;\frac{1-b}{\frac{1}{\lambda}+a}.

(A.13)

(Indeed, the first inequality is equivalent to
\lambda_{*}\geq((1-b)/\lambda^{\prime}-a)^{-1}
, and the second one gives an upper bound; (
A.9
) is precisely the condition that the interval is nonempty.)

Equip
\mathbb{C}^{2}
with the anisotropic norm
\|(x,y)\|^{(\lambda_{*})}:=\lambda_{*}|x|+|y|
. For
(x,y),(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
, Lemma
A.1
(with
(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda)\leftarrow(\lambda_{*},\lambda)
) and the mean value theorem give, for
j=1,2
,

\displaystyle|\xi_{j}(x,y)-\xi_{j}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle|\xi_{j}(x,y)-\xi_{j}(x,y^{\prime})|+|\xi_{j}(x,y^{\prime})-\xi_{j}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|y-y^{\prime}|\left|{\frac{\partial\xi_{j}}{\partial y}}\right|^{(\lambda_{*})}+|x-x^{\prime}|\left|{\frac{\partial\xi_{j}}{\partial x}}\right|^{(\lambda_{*})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|y-y^{\prime}|\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}|\bar{\xi}_{j}|^{(\lambda)}+|x-x^{\prime}|\frac{\lambda\lambda_{*}}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}|\bar{\xi}_{j}|^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}|\bar{\xi}_{j}|^{(\lambda)}\|(x-x^{\prime},y-y^{\prime})\|^{(\lambda_{*})}.

Hence

\displaystyle\|\Xi(x,y)-\Xi(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\|^{(\lambda_{*})}=

\displaystyle\lambda_{*}|\xi_{1}(x,y)-\xi_{1}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|+|\xi_{2}(x,y)-\xi_{2}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}\,\bigl(\lambda_{*}a+b\bigr)\,\|(x-x^{\prime},y-y^{\prime})\|^{(\lambda_{*})}

By the second inequality in (
A.13
),
\lambda_{*}a+b<1-\lambda_{*}/\lambda
, so the last line is
<\|(x-x^{\prime},y-y^{\prime})\|^{(\lambda_{*})}
. Thus
\Xi
is a contraction on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
for the distance induced by
\|\cdot\|^{(\lambda_{*})}
and, in particular,
\id+\Xi
is injective there.
Moreover, since the determinant of the Jacobian matrix of
\id+\Xi
at
(x,y)
writes

\det(d_{(x,y)}(\id+\Xi))=\Big(1+\frac{\partial\xi_{1}}{\partial x}(x,y)\Big)\Big(1+\frac{\partial\xi_{2}}{\partial y}(x,y)\Big)-\frac{\partial\xi_{1}}{\partial y}(x,y)\frac{\partial\xi_{2}}{\partial x}(x,y),

by (
A.3
) again,
we have for
(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
that

\displaystyle|\det(d_{(x,y)}(\id+\Xi))|\geq

\displaystyle\Big(1-\left|{\frac{\partial\xi_{1}}{\partial x}}\right|_{\lambda_{*}}\Big)\Big(1-\left|{\frac{\partial\xi_{2}}{\partial y}}\right|_{\lambda_{*}}\Big)-\left|{\frac{\partial\xi_{1}}{\partial y}\frac{\partial\xi_{2}}{\partial x}}\right|_{\lambda_{*}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Big(1-\frac{\lambda\lambda_{*}}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}|\bar{\xi}_{1}|_{\lambda}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}|\bar{\xi}_{2}|_{\lambda}\Big)-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}|\bar{\xi}_{1}|_{\lambda}\frac{\lambda\lambda_{*}}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}|\bar{\xi}_{2}|_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-\lambda_{*}}(\lambda_{*}|\bar{\xi}_{1}|_{\lambda}+|\bar{\xi}_{2}|_{\lambda})>0.

Hence,
\id+\Xi
is a diffeomorphism from
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
onto its image.

Next, we show
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\subset(\id+\Xi)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}})
. It suffices to check that
(\id+\Xi)
maps the boundary
\partial\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
outside the interior of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
and then appeal to degree/invariance-of-domain for the injective homotopy
\id+t\Xi
,
t\in[0,1]
. Let
(x,y)\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
, so
|\Im x|+|y|/\lambda_{*}=1
. Using
|\xi_{1}|\leq|y|\,a
and
|\xi_{2}|\leq|y|\,b
, we estimate

\displaystyle|\Im(x+\xi_{1})|+\frac{|y+\xi_{2}|}{\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq|\Im x|+\frac{|y|}{\lambda^{\prime}}-|y|\,a-\frac{|y|}{\lambda^{\prime}}\,b

\displaystyle\geq|\Im x|+\frac{|y|}{\lambda_{*}}\,\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{*}}{\lambda^{\prime}}-\lambda_{*}a-\frac{\lambda_{*}}{\lambda^{\prime}}\,b\Bigr).

Writing
s:=|\Im x|
and
t:=|y|/\lambda_{*}
(so
s+t=1
) gives

s+(1-s)\,\lambda_{*}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}-a-\frac{b}{\lambda^{\prime}}\Bigr)\;\geq\;\min\!\Bigl\{1,\,\lambda_{*}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}-a-\frac{b}{\lambda^{\prime}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

By the first inequality in (
A.13
),
\lambda_{*}\bigl(\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}-a-\frac{b}{\lambda^{\prime}}\bigr)\geq 1
, hence the minimum is
1
. Thus
(\id+\Xi)(\partial\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}})
lies outside the interior of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, and consequently
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\subset(\id+\Xi)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}})
.
Since
\id+\Xi
is injective on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
, we can define
\id+\Sigma:\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
satisfying (
A.10
).
By construction,
\Sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies
\sigma_{j}(x,0)=0
, so there are analytic
\bar{\sigma}_{j}
with
\sigma_{j}(x,y)=y\,\bar{\sigma}_{j}(x,y)
.

Suppose
0<\lambda^{\prime\prime}<\lambda^{\prime}
such that
(\id+\Xi)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.
Then restricting (
A.10
) to
(\id+\Xi)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})
yields
(\id+\Xi)\circ(\id+\Sigma)\circ(\id+\Xi)=(\id+\Xi)
on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
.
Since
(\id+\Sigma)\circ(\id+\Xi)(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})\subset\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
by construction of
\Sigma
and
(\id+\Xi)
injective on
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda_{*}}
, we obtain (
A.11
).

It remains to prove (
A.12
). From
(\id+\Xi)\circ(\id+\Sigma)=\id
we get
\Sigma+\Xi\circ(\id+\Sigma)=0
, which yields

\bar{\sigma}_{j}\;=\;-\,\frac{\bar{\xi}_{j}\circ(\id+\Sigma)}{1+\bar{\xi}_{2}\circ(\id+\Sigma)}\qquad(j=1,2).

Define
v_{j}(x,y):=-\bar{\xi}_{j}(x,y)/(1+\bar{\xi}_{2}(x,y))
. By repeated differentiation (quotient rule) one obtains, for each
r\geq 0
,

|v_{1}|^{(\lambda,r)}+|v_{2}|^{(\lambda,r)}\;\leq\;D_{r}\,\Biggl(\frac{1+|\bar{\xi}_{1}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}+|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}}{1-|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda)}}\Biggr)^{r+1}\Bigl(|\bar{\xi}_{1}|^{(\lambda,r)}+|\bar{\xi}_{2}|^{(\lambda,r)}\Bigr)

for some constant
D_{r}>0
depending only on
r
. Since
\bar{\sigma}_{j}=v_{j}\circ(\id+\Sigma)
, Proposition
A.2
(3) gives, for
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\lambda^{\prime})
and
\beta=(\lambda^{\prime\prime}/\lambda^{\prime})^{1/r}
,

1+|\bar{\sigma}_{1}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}+|\bar{\sigma}_{2}|^{(\lambda^{\prime\prime},r)}\;\leq\;C_{r}\Bigl(\frac{r+\lambda^{\prime}}{1-\beta}\Bigr)^{A_{r}}\Bigl(1+|v_{1}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}+|v_{2}|^{(\lambda,r-1)}\Bigr)^{B_{r}}\Bigl(1+|v_{1}|^{(\lambda,r)}+|v_{2}|^{(\lambda,r)}\Bigr).

Substituting the bounds for
v_{j}
and enlarging constants completes the proof of (
A.12
).
∎

Appendix B
A characterization of periodic orbits.

Let
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
and
a,b>0
.
Consider an analytic map

F:\binom{x}{y}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}x+\omega+y+f(x,y)\\
y+g(x,y)\end{pmatrix},\qquad f,g:\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}\to\mathbb{C}.

(B.1)

For iterates we write

F^{j}(x,y)\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}x+j\omega+jy+f_{j}(x,y)\\
y+g_{j}(x,y)\end{pmatrix},

(B.2)

whenever the right-hand side is defined. From (
B.1
),

f_{0}=g_{0}=0,\quad f_{1}=f,\quad g_{1}=g,\qquad\begin{cases}f_{j+1}=f_{j}+g_{j}+f\circ F^{j},\\
g_{j+1}=g_{j}+g\circ F^{j}.\end{cases}

(B.3)

The next lemma gives a domain of definition and sup-norm bounds for
f_{j},g_{j}
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
f,g
be analytic on
\mathbb{T}_{a}\times\mathbb{D}_{b}
and let
f_{j},g_{j}
be as in (
B.2
).
Fix
a^{\prime}<a
,
b^{\prime}<b
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. If

\displaystyle a^{\prime}\;+\;q\,b^{\prime}\;+\;q\,|f|_{a,b}\;+\;\frac{q(q-1)}{2}\,|g|_{a,b}

\displaystyle\leq a,

(B.4)

\displaystyle b^{\prime}\;+\;q\,|g|_{a,b}

\displaystyle\leq b,

then, for
0\leq j\leq q
, the functions
f_{j},g_{j}
are well-defined on
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
and

|g_{j}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq j\,|g|_{a,b},\qquad|f_{j}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq j\,|f|_{a,b}+\frac{j(j-1)}{2}\,|g|_{a,b}.

(B.5)

Proof.

Induction on
j
. The case
j=0
is trivial since
F^{0}=\id
.
Assume
0\leq j<q
and
(x_{j},y_{j}):=F^{j}(x,y)
is defined for every
(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
with (
B.5
).
Then

|y_{j}|\leq b^{\prime}+j\,|g|_{a,b},\qquad|\Im x_{j}|\leq a^{\prime}+j\,b^{\prime}+j\,|f|_{a,b}+\frac{j(j-1)}{2}\,|g|_{a,b}.

By (
B.4
),
|y_{j}|<b
and
|\Im x_{j}|<a
, so the compositions in (
B.3
) are well-defined; hence
f_{j+1}
and
g_{j+1}
are well-defined there.
Taking suprema in (
B.3
) and using
|f\circ F^{j}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq|f|_{a,b},
and
|g\circ F^{j}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq|g|_{a,b}
, we obtain

\displaystyle|g_{j+1}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq|g_{j}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}+|g|_{a,b}\leq(j+1)\,|g|_{a,b},

\displaystyle|f_{j+1}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq|f_{j}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}+|g_{j}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}+|f|_{a,b}\leq(j+1)\,|f|_{a,b}+\frac{j(j+1)}{2}\,|g|_{a,b},

which is exactly (
B.5
) with
j
replaced by
j+1
. This completes the induction
∎

From now on, set

\omega=\frac{p}{q}

for some coprime
(p,q)
with
q>0
, and assume
|f|_{a,b}
,
|g|_{a,b}
are small enough that Lemma
B.1
applies for some
a^{\prime}<a
,
b^{\prime}<b
.
In particular,
f_{q}
and
g_{q}
are defined on
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
via (
B.3
).
By (
B.2
), a point
(x,y)
belongs to a
(p,q)
–periodic orbit of
F
iff

\begin{cases}qy+f_{q}(x,y)=0,\\
g_{q}(x,y)=0\end{cases}

for some lift of
f_{q}
to the universal cover.
To solve the first equation we look for
\gamma:\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}
such that

\gamma(x)=-\frac{1}{q}\,f_{q}(x,\gamma(x)).

Then it suffices to find
x_{0}
with

g_{q}(x_{0},\gamma(x_{0}))=0.

The point
(x_{0},\gamma(x_{0}))
will be a
(p,q)
-orbit under
F
.
Let us formalize this argument with quantitative details in the next lemma.

Lemma B.2
.

Consider a map
F
of the form (
B.1
) with
\omega=p/q
and suppose, for some
a^{\prime}<a
, that

b+|f|_{a,b}\leq\frac{a-a^{\prime}}{q}\quad\text{and}\quad|f|_{a,b}+\frac{3q-1}{2}\,|g|_{a,b}<b.

(B.6)

Put
b^{\prime}:=b-q|g|_{a,b}
and
b^{\prime\prime}=|f|_{a,b}+\frac{q-1}{2}|g|_{a,b}
.
Then all
(p,q)
-periodic orbits of
F|_{\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}}
lie in the strip
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime\prime}}
.
More precisely, there exist analytic functions
\gamma,\Gamma:\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}
depending analytically on
f
and
g
with

|\gamma|_{a^{\prime}}\leq|f|_{a,b}+\frac{q-1}{2}|g|_{a,b},\qquad|\Gamma|_{a^{\prime}}\leq q\,|g|_{a,b}

(B.7)

such that the following holds.
For
(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
, the point
(x,y)
is a
(p,q)
–periodic orbit of
F
iff

y=\gamma(x)\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma(x)=0.

Moreover, with
\bar{a}=2q|f|_{a,b}+q(q-1)|g|_{a,b}
and
\bar{b}=|f|_{a,b}+(3q-1)|g|_{a,b}/2
, we have

\left|{\Gamma-\sum_{k=0}^{q-1}g\Big(\cdot+\frac{k}{q},0\Big)}\right|_{a^{\prime}}\leq q\Big(\frac{\bar{a}}{a-a^{\prime}-\bar{a}}+\frac{\bar{b}}{b-\bar{b}}\Big)|g|_{a,b}

(B.8)

and

\Bigl|\det\!\bigl(d_{(x,\gamma(x))}F^{q}-\id\bigr)+q\,\dot{\Gamma}(x)\Bigr|\leq\frac{\bar{a}}{2}\frac{|\dot{\Gamma}(x)|}{b^{\prime}-|\gamma(x)|}.

(B.9)

Proof.

Define
b^{\prime}=b-q|g|_{a,b}
as in the statement.
The second inequality of condition (
B.6
) imply
q|g|_{a,b}<b
, hence
b^{\prime}>0
. Using the first inequality in (
B.6
) and definition of
b^{\prime}
,

a^{\prime}+qb^{\prime}+q|f|_{a,b}+\frac{q(q-1)}{2}|g|_{a,b}\leq a^{\prime}+qb+q|f|_{a,b}\leq a,

so Lemma
B.1
applies, and
f_{q},g_{q}
are defined on
\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
with

|f_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq q|f|_{a,b}+\frac{q(q-1)}{2}|g|_{a,b},\qquad|g_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq q|g|_{a,b}.

(B.10)

By the second inequality in (
B.6
),

|f|_{a,b}+\frac{q-1}{2}|g|_{a,b}\;<\;b-q|g|_{a,b}\;=\;b^{\prime},

so for each fixed
x\in\mathbb{T}_{a^{\prime}}
, on
|z|=b^{\prime}
we have

\Bigl|\frac{1}{q}f_{q}(x,z)\Bigr|\;\leq\;\frac{1}{q}|f_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\;<\;b^{\prime}\;=\;|z|.

By Rouché’s theorem,
z\mapsto z+\frac{1}{q}f_{q}(x,z)
has exactly one zero in
\mathbb{D}_{b^{\prime}}
, denoted
\gamma(x)
, depending analytically on
x
.
The bound (
B.7
) on
|\gamma|_{a^{\prime}}
follows from
\gamma(x)=-f_{q}(x,\gamma(x))/q
and (
B.10
).
Define
\Gamma(x):=g_{q}(x,\gamma(x))
, so
|\Gamma|_{a^{\prime}}\leq|g_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\leq q|g|_{a,b}
.

By construction, the lift of
F
and
(x,\gamma(x))
into the universal cover satisfies

F^{q}(x,\gamma(x))=\bigl(x+p,\,\gamma(x)+\Gamma(x)\bigr),

(B.11)

and uniqueness of the zero implies the first coordinate of
F^{q}(x,y^{\prime})
equals
x+p
iff
y^{\prime}=\gamma(x)
; thus
(x,y)
is a
(p,q)
-orbit under
F
iff
y=\gamma(x)
and
\Gamma(x)=0
.

For (
B.8
), we have by (
B.3
) that

\Gamma(x)=g_{q}(x,\gamma(x))=\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}g\circ F^{j}(x,\gamma(x))

where, by (
B.2
) with
\omega=p/q
,

g\circ F^{j}(x,\gamma(x))=g(x+jp/q+j\gamma(x)+f_{j}(x,\gamma(x)),\gamma(x)+g_{j}(x,\gamma(x))).

Since
j\leq q
, the estimates (
B.7
) and (
B.5
) imply that

|j\gamma(x)+f_{j}(x,\gamma(x))|\leq\bar{a};\qquad|\gamma(x)+g_{j}(x,\gamma(x))|\leq\bar{b}.

Hence, by the mean value theorem and Cauchy’s estimate,

\displaystyle|g\circ F^{j}(x,\gamma(x))-g(x+jp/q,0)|\leq\left|{\frac{\partial g}{\partial x}}\right|_{a^{\prime}+\bar{a},\bar{b}}\bar{a}+\left|{\frac{\partial g}{\partial y}}\right|_{a^{\prime}+\bar{a},\bar{b}}\bar{b}\leq\Big(\frac{\bar{a}}{a-a^{\prime}-\bar{a}}+\frac{\bar{b}}{b-\bar{b}}\Big)|g|_{a,b}.

Summing over
j
, we obtain (
B.8
).

For (
B.9
), differentiate (
B.11
) to get

\frac{d}{dt}F^{q}(x+t,\gamma(x+t))\big|_{t=0}=(1,\dot{\gamma}(x)+\dot{\Gamma}(x)).

Also,

\frac{\partial F^{q}}{\partial y}(x,\gamma(x))=\Big(q+\frac{\partial f_{q}}{\partial y}(x,\gamma(x)),1+\frac{\partial g_{q}}{\partial y}(x,\gamma(x))\Big)

In the basis
\{(1,\dot{\gamma}(x)),(0,1)\}
the Jacobian is

d_{(x,\gamma(x))}F^{q}=\begin{pmatrix}1&q+\frac{\partial f_{q}}{\partial y}(x,\gamma(x))\\
\dot{\Gamma}(x)&1+\frac{\partial g_{q}}{\partial y}(x,\gamma(x))-\dot{\gamma}(x)\left(q+\frac{\partial f_{q}}{\partial y}(x,\gamma(x))\right)\end{pmatrix}.

A direct computation yields

\det\!\bigl(d_{(x,\gamma(x))}F^{q}-\id\bigr)+q\,\dot{\Gamma}(x)\;=\;-\,\dot{\Gamma}(x)\,\partial_{y}f_{q}(x,\gamma(x)).

Cauchy’s estimate and (
B.10
) give

|\partial_{y}f_{q}(x,\gamma(x))|\;\leq\;\frac{|f_{q}|_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}}{\,b^{\prime}-|\gamma(x)|\,}\;\leq\;\frac{q|f|_{a,b}+\frac{q(q-1)}{2}|g|_{a,b}}{\,b^{\prime}-|\gamma(x)|\,},

which implies (
B.9
).
∎

Appendix C
Properties of the Fourier-weighted norm

Recall that for
u:\mathbb{T}_{a}\to\mathbb{C}
analytic, the Fourier-weighted norm
\|u\|_{a}
is defined to be

\|u\|_{a}:=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|\hat{u}_{k}|\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|k|},

where
\hat{u}_{k}
is the
k
th Fourier coefficient of
u
.
In this appendix, we collect some elementary properties of this norm used in the article.

Lemma C.1
.

For fixed
a>0
, the space of analytic functions
f:\mathbb{T}_{a}\to\mathbb{C}
with
\|f\|_{a}<\infty
is a Banach algebra under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{a}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show
\|fg\|_{a}\leq\|f\|_{a}\|g\|_{a}
.
Recall that the Fourier coefficient
\widehat{fg}_{n}
of the product function
fg
can be given in terms of a convolution
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{n-k}\widehat{g}_{k}
.
To prove the lemma, the key observation is the triangle inequality
|n|\leq|n-k|+|k|
and the following computation:

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}|\widehat{fg}_{n}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|n|}\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|\widehat{f}_{\,n-k}|\,|\widehat{g}_{k}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|n|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|\widehat{f}_{\,n-k}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|n-k|}\;|\widehat{g}_{k}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|k|}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}|\widehat{f}_{\,n-k}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|n-k|}\Big)\,|\widehat{g}_{k}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|k|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}|\widehat{f}_{m}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|m|}\Big)\,|\widehat{g}_{k}|\,\mathrm{e}^{2\pi a|k|}=\|f\|_{a}\,\|g\|_{a}.

The interchange of sums is justified since all series involve non-negative terms and converge absolutely. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
.
For
u:\mathbb{T}_{a}\to\mathbb{C}
analytic, define
\mathcal{D}_{\delta}u(x):=u(x+\delta)-u(x)
.
Then the function
\mathcal{D}_{\delta}u
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\delta}u\right\|_{a}\leq

\displaystyle|\delta|\left\|\dot{u}\right\|_{a}

(C.1)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\delta}u-\delta\dot{u}\right\|_{a}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left|{\delta}\right|^{2}\left\|\ddot{u}\right\|_{a}.

(C.2)

Proof.

Since
|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta}-1|\leq|\theta|
for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\hat{\dot{u}}_{k}=2\pi\mathrm{i}k\hat{u}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\delta}u\right\|_{a}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\left|\left(e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k\delta}-1\right)\hat{u}_{k}\right|e^{2\pi a|k|}\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|\delta|\left|2\pi k\hat{u}_{k}\right|e^{2\pi a|k|}=|\delta|\left\|\dot{u}\right\|_{a}.

On the other hand, since
|e^{\mathrm{i}\theta}-1-\mathrm{i}\theta|\leq\theta^{2}/2
for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\hat{\ddot{u}}_{k}=(2\pi\mathrm{i}k)^{2}\hat{u}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\delta}u-\delta\dot{u}\right\|_{a}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\left|\left(e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k\delta}-1-2\pi\mathrm{i}k\delta\right)\hat{u}_{k}\right|e^{2\pi a|k|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{2}\left|{\delta}\right|^{2}|2\pi k|^{2}\left|\hat{u}_{k}\right|e^{2\pi a|k|}=\frac{1}{2}\left|{\delta}\right|^{2}\left\|\ddot{u}\right\|_{a}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma C.3

(Fourier separation lemma)
.

Suppose
M>N
and
u,f:\mathbb{T}_{a}\to\mathbb{C}
are analytic functions. Then we have

\left\|\left\langle{f\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|_{a}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{a}\left\|\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{M}+\Big(\frac{2N}{q}+1\Big)\mathrm{e}^{-4\pi a(M-N)}\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{\geq M}\right\|_{a}.

(C.3)

Proof.

Using
|k|-|n|\leq|n-k|\leq|k|+|n|
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle{f\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{\geq M}}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|_{a}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid n\\
|n|\leq N\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid k\\
|k|\geq M\end{subarray}}\left|{\hat{f}_{n-k}\hat{u}_{k}}\right|e^{2\pi a|n|}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid n\\
|n|\leq N\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid k\\
|k|\geq M\end{subarray}}\left|{f}\right|_{a}e^{-2\pi a|n-k|}\left|{\hat{u}_{k}}\right|e^{2\pi a|n|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid n\\
|n|\leq N\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid k\\
|k|\geq M\end{subarray}}\left|{f}\right|_{a}e^{-2\pi a|n-k|}e^{2\pi a(|n|-|k|)}\left|{\hat{u}_{k}}\right|e^{2\pi a|k|}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid n\\
|n|\leq N\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid k\\
|k|\geq M\end{subarray}}\left|{f}\right|_{a}e^{-4\pi a(|k|-|n|)}\left|{\hat{u}_{k}}\right|e^{2\pi a|k|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{2N}{q}+1\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\mid k\\
|k|\geq M\end{subarray}}\left|{f}\right|_{a}e^{-4\pi a(M-N)}\left|{\hat{u}_{k}}\right|e^{2\pi a|k|}\leq\Big(\frac{2N}{q}+1\Big)e^{-4\pi a(M-N)}\left|{f}\right|_{a}\left\|\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{\geq M}\right\|_{a}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle{f\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|_{a}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left\langle{f\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{M}}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|_{a}+\left\|\left\langle{f\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{\geq M}}\right\rangle_{q}^{N}\right\|_{a}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{a}\left(\left\|\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{M}\right\|_{a}+\Big(\frac{2N}{q}+1\Big)e^{-4\pi a(M-N)}\left\|\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{\geq M}\right\|_{a}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{a}\left\|\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{M}+\Big(\frac{2N}{q}+1\Big)e^{-4\pi a(M-N)}\left\langle{u}\right\rangle_{q}^{\geq M}\right\|_{a}

which proves (
C.3
).
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎
